*Ghālibūn* (victors) — the promise's scope across revelation needs surveying, for triumphalism and despair both misread it. *The guarantee-texts*: *"Our word preceded to Our servants, the messengers: they shall be helped, Our host shall be victors"* (37:171–173); *"God decreed: I and My messengers shall prevail"* (58:21); *"God helps those helping Him"* (22:40); *"how many small companies overcame large ones, by God's leave"* (2:249). *The conditions*: helping God (22:40–41: prayer, alms, commanding right), patience-and-reverence (3:120, 200; 8:46), unity (8:46: *"dispute not, lest you fail"*), preparation (8:60). *The forms*: military (Badr, conquests), argumentative (Abraham's debates, 2:258; 21:58–70), civilizational (Islam's spread), eschatological (martyrs' triumph, 3:169–171), personal (soul's victory over caprice). *The delays*: Uḥud's wound (3:152, 165), Ḥunayn's rout-then-triumph (9:25–26), the Hour's deferral (martyrs awaiting full vindication). The synthesis: victory *guaranteed* (identity), *conditioned* (means), *diversified* (forms), *deferred* (timing). Hedgers (v. 52) demanded immediate military certainty; the verse offers ultimate cosmic certainty. Invest accordingly: strive conditionally, trust unconditionally, await patiently. The party wins; the King cannot lose.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:57

> **O you who believe! Take not as allies those who take your religion as mockery and play, be they among those who were given the Book before you or the disbelievers. And reverence God, if you are believers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Mockery as Disqualifier: Intimacy's Red Line**

*Take not as allies those who take your religion as mockery* (*huzuwan*) *and play* (*laʿiban*) — *whether from the scriptured or the pagans*. Mockery (*istihzā'*) of religion recurs as the Qur'an's intimacy-red-line (4:140; 6:68 — *do not sit with mockers until they change subject*). The ruling's wisdom is psychological before it is political: intimacy with those who despise your deepest commitments corrodes from within — one cannot confide in contempt. The verse does not command severing all contact (commerce, neighborliness, and justice continue — 60:8), but bars *walāyah*-intimacy: confiding secrets, seeking patronage, taking as inner circle. *Reverence God, if you are believers* — *taqwā* as the test of sincerity: true belief cannot cozy up to its own mockery. The believer who laughs along when his religion is ridiculed, to keep the room comfortable, has failed exactly this verse. Dignity has a price; the verse names it — reverence over approval.

**Istihzā': Mockery's Qur'anic Profile**

*Istihzā'* (mockery) of revelation — the verse's disqualifier — profiles across the Qur'an as disbelief's *signature behavior*, and the dossier needs compiling. *Its targets*: messengers (15:11: *"no messenger came but they mocked him"*; 21:36, 41; 25:41), revelations (6:10; 16:34; 40:83), believers (2:14: *"we are but mockers"*; 83:29–30: laughing at believers), resurrection (37:12–14; 45:32–33), rituals (this verse's *ṣalāh*-mockery, v. 58). *Its agents*: every disbelieving elite — Noah's chiefs (11:38), Hūd's ʿĀd (7:66), Ṣāliḥ's Thamūd (7:77), Shuʿayb's Madyan (11:87), Pharaoh's court (43:51–52), Quraysh (25:41). *Its wages*: encirclement-by-mocked (6:10; 16:34: *"what they mocked encompassed them"*), Hell's mockery-returned (83:34–36: believers laughing at disbelievers, on thrones watching). *The believer's protocol*: non-sitting (4:140; 6:68 — withdraw till subject changes), non-intimacy (this verse), non-imitation (never mock others' sacred — 6:108's restraint), dignified response (25:63: *"peace"* to the ignorant; 28:55: *"to us our deeds, to you yours"*). Mockery disqualifies from *walāyah* because contempt precludes trust: the mocker cannot be patron, confidant, or ally — only contact, counterpart, or caller-to-truth. Red line drawn; dignity priced; protocol issued.

**Dignity Under Ridicule: The Believer's Bearing**

The verse's *ittaqū'Llāh* (reverence God) under mockery-pressure prescribes the believer's *bearing* — and the bearing's models need exhibiting. *The Prophet's ﷺ bearing*: mocked as poet, madman, sorcerer (37:36; 68:51; 74:24) — responding with persistence, never retaliation-in-kind; Ṭā'if's blood answered with the mountains-offer refused (mercy over vengeance); Quraysh's boycott answered with patience. *The Companions' bearing*: Bilāl under torture — *"Aḥad, Aḥad"* (One, One) — dignity in one word; Khabbāb's back branded — endurance testifying; the Makkan poor mocked (83:29–30) — promised laughter's reversal (83:34–36). *The protocol's tiers*: withdraw (4:140; 6:68 — leave mocking sessions), witness (calm correction where receptivity glimmers), withstand (patience where hostility reigns), never *wilt* (laughing-along, apologizing-for-truth, trimming-faith-for-approval — this verse's failure-mode). *The interior dimension*: mockery *tests* reverence — *ittaqū'Llāh in kuntum mu'minīn* (reverence God *if believers*) — faith proven where approval costs truth. The believer's bearing under ridicule: unbowed head, unsealed lips (truth spoken), unclenched fist (retaliation withheld), unbroken stride (path continued). Dignity's price is reverence; the verse collects gladly.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:58

> **And when you call to prayer, they take it as mockery and play. That is because they are a people who understand not.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Mocking the Call: The Adhān Under Ridicule**

*When you call to prayer* (*nādaytum ila'ṣ-ṣalāh* — the *adhān*) *they take it as mockery and play*. The reports specify Jewish contemporaries mocking the call (Ṭ) — some adding Christians (Z) — laughing at the public proclamation of *Allāhu akbar*. The *adhān*'s mockery-worthiness, from the mocker's view, is precisely its shamelessness: five times daily, from rooftops, the community announces its Master's greatness to the indifferent city. Mockery is the tribute vice pays to unembarrassability. *That is because they are a people who understand not* (*lā yaʿqilūn*) — mockery of worship is diagnosed as intellectual failure, not sophistication: they cannot *reason* (*ʿaql*) what prayer is — the creature's audience with its Creator — so they laugh at what transcends them. The verse comforts every believer whose devotion draws smirks: the smirk testifies against the smirker. Understanding would produce reverence or at least respect; laughter at prayer is confessed incomprehension.

**The Adhān: Heaven's Call and Its Honors**

*Nidā'* (call) *to prayer* — the mocked *adhān* — carries honors the mockers cannot fathom, and exhibiting them answers mockery with majesty. *Its origin*: shown to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Zayd in dream, confirmed by ʿUmar's matching dream, instituted by the Prophet ﷺ (Abū Dāwūd 498–499; al-Tirmidhī 189) — dream-vision ratified by revelation's silence (approval). *Its text*: *takbīr* (God's greatness, opening-and-closing), *shahādatayn* (the two testimonies — faith's essence broadcast), *ḥayʿalah* (come-to-prayer, come-to-success — invitation universal), *tathwīb* (dawn's *"prayer is better than sleep"* — priority declared). *Its virtues*: Satan flees its sound (al-Bukhārī 608; Muslim 389 — *till inaudible*); its hearers testify for the caller (al-Bukhārī 609: *"no jinn, man, or thing hears the caller's voice but testifies for him"*); its caller is foremost-necked on Resurrection Day (Muslim 387 — *longest necks*, honor displayed); its words answered earn Paradise (Muslim 385 — *sincerely from heart*). *Its mockery* (this verse): incomprehension's confession — *lā yaʿqilūn*. The mocked call outlives every mocker: fourteen centuries, five times daily, every continent — the smirked-at *takbīr* now echoing where mockers' empires lie buried. Answer mockery by calling louder: *Allāhu akbar* — understood by the understanding, laughed by the laughing, triumphant over all.

**Lā Yaʿqilūn: Incomprehension as Moral Verdict**

*Lā yaʿqilūn* — "they understand not" (*ʿaql*: reason, intellect, restraint): mockery diagnosed as *cognitive* failure — and the Qur'an's *ʿaql*-theology needs stating, for it inverts the mocker's self-image. The mocker imagines sophistication (enlightened amusement at primitive ritual); revelation diagnoses *mindlessness* — *ʿaql* absent. The Qur'an's *lā yaʿqilūn*-catalogue: pagans following forefathers blindly (2:170–171: *"deaf, dumb, blind — they understand not"*), ritualists without comprehension (5:103's *baḥīrah-sā'ibah* custodians), mockers (this verse), mandate-resisters (8:22: *"worst creatures: deaf, dumb, who understand not"*), majority-opinionists (29:63: *"most understand not"*). *ʿAql* in Qur'anic usage is *moral* reason — intellect *restraining* (*ʿaqala*: to bind, hobble) impulse toward truth: the *ʿāqil* binds desire by evidence; the *ghayr-ʿāqil* unbinds impulse against evidence. Mockery of prayer is thus *unbound* impulse (contempt, conformity, cruelty) overriding *bindable* evidence (prayer's universality, profundity, fruits). True sophistication *studies* before sneering; *ʿaql* investigates before laughing. The verse's verdict stands: sneering at the sublime is not intellect's triumph but intellect's absence. *Lā yaʿqilūn* — the smirk self-diagnosed.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:59

> **Say, "O People of the Book! Do you resent us for aught but that we believe in God, and what was sent down unto us and what was sent down before, and that most of you are iniquitous?"**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Challenge of Resentment: What Exactly Do You Hate?**

*Say: O People of the Book, do you resent us* (*tanqimūn* — revenge, resent; cf. 7:126; 85:8) *for aught but that we believe in God, what was sent down to us, what was sent down before — and that most of you are iniquitous?* The challenge forces the hostile to itemize their grievance: strip away politics, tribalism, and envy — what *theological* complaint remains? That Muslims believe in God? In the Qur'an? In the Torah and Gospel themselves? The resentment's emptiness, once itemized, indicts itself. The closing clause — *most of you are fāsiqūn* (iniquitous, cf. vv. 47, 49) — adds the mirror: the resented are the believers; the resenters are largely the law-breakers. Persecution's psychology is reversed: the persecutors claim offense, but the offended are the righteous whose only crime is comprehensive belief. The verse arms every maligned believer with the question: *name the crime*. Belief in God? In all revelations? The indictment collapses into confession.

**Naqmah: Resentment's Anatomy in Revelation**

*Tanqimūn* (you resent, take vengeance) — from *naqmah* (vengeance, vengeful resentment): the verse probes persecution's *motive*, and revelation's *naqmah*-dossier exposes it fully. *Pharaoh's magicians*: *"you resent us only for believing our Lord's signs when they came"* (7:126) — belief the sole crime, vengeance the sole motive. *The Trench-people* (*Aṣḥāb al-Ukhdūd*): *"they resented them only for believing in God, the Mighty, Praiseworthy"* (85:8) — furnace-persecution for faith-alone. *This verse*: resentment for comprehensive belief (God, Qur'an, prior scriptures). *The pattern*: persecutors *claim* crimes (sedition, corruption, deviance) while revelation *exposes* the sole crime — belief. *Naqmah*'s psychology: vengefulness toward *unanswerable* righteousness — the resenters cannot refute (evidences clear), cannot match (virtue evident), so they *punish* — vengeance as argument's substitute. *The Qur'an's counter*: force itemization — *name the crime* (this verse's challenge); history vindicates (magicians martyred-triumphant, Trench-people Gardens-promised, Muslims conquest-vindicated). Every maligned believer inherits the challenge: persecutors, *itemize*. Belief-in-God? Belief-in-all-Books? The indictment collapses; the confession stands. *Naqmah* unmasked is *naqmah* disarmed.

**Belief in All Revelations: Islam's Comprehensive Confession**

*Nu'minu bi'Llāh, wa mā unzila ilaynā, wa mā unzila min qabl* — "we believe in God, what was sent to us, what was sent before": Islam's comprehensive confession — and its *exclusivity-inclusivity* balance needs articulating, for the verse wields it as resentment's answer. *The confession*: Muslims believe *all* genuine revelation — Torah, Psalms, Gospel, Qur'an, and the Scrolls (87:18–19) — honoring every messenger (2:285: *"no distinction among messengers"*). *The criterion*: the Qur'an *guards* (*muḥaymin*, v. 48) — confirming authentic strata, correcting corruptions. *The resentment's irony*: People-of-Book resenting Muslims *for believing their own Books* (plus the Qur'an) — resenting *inclusion* of their scriptures. *The parallel*: Quraysh resenting Muslims for honoring Abraham (2:135's *ḥanīfiyyah*-claim); Jews resenting converts for believing Moses *truly* (Ibn Salām's reception — v. 41's portrait). *The apologetic power*: Islam cannot be accused of revelation-rejection — it out-believes its accusers, affirming *their* Books *plus* its own. Resent *that*: belief too comprehensive, reverence too inclusive. The verse's challenge stands eternally: *tanqimūna... illā an āmannā* — you resent *nothing but our believing*. Name another crime; the silence convicts.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:60

> **Say, "Shall I inform you of those who shall have a worse recompense from God than that? Those whom God has cursed and with whom He is wrath, and made some of them apes and swine, and worshippers of idols. Those are worse in station and further astray from the even path."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Worse Than That": The Mirror Turned on the Mockers**

*Shall I inform you of worse recompense from God than that* — "that" being the Muslims' supposed crime of belief (v. 59)? The mirror turns: the mockers' own history contains Divine wrath's specimens — *those God cursed* (*laʿana*), *with whom He is wrath* (*ghaḍiba*), *made some apes and swine* (*qiradah wa khanāzīr* — Sabbath-violators transformed, 2:65; 7:166; swine per some reports (Ṭ)), *and worshippers of ṭāghūt* (idols, tyrants, false gods; cf. 2:256–257; 4:51, 60, 76; 16:36; 39:17). *Those are worse in station* (*sharrun makānan*) *and further astray from the even path* (*sawā' al-sabīl*). The argument is *ad hominem* in the classical, legitimate sense: you mock our belief — examine your record. The verse does not generalize to all Jews or Christians (the Qur'an praises the righteous among them — 3:113–114; 5:69; 7:159); it cites *specific* historical judgments as warnings. Polemic here serves diagnosis: communities that whitewash their past cannot diagnose their present. The verse prescribes historical honesty as the prerequisite of theological debate.

**Apes and Swine: The Transformation Reports**

*Jaʿala minhumu'l-qiradata wa'l-khanāzīra* — "made some of them apes and swine": the transformation (*maskh*) reports need careful handling, for the verse's plain sense is contested and its polemical abuse rampant. *The ape-transformation*: Sabbath-violators of Aylah (2:65: *"be apes, despised"*; 7:163–166: detailed narrative — Sabbath-fishing stratagem, warners ignored, *"be apes, outcast"*). *The swine*: some reports extend *maskh* to swine for other violations (Ṭ) — Jesus' table-denier traditions (v. 115's threatened punishment), or general rebellion. *Literal versus metaphorical*: the mainstream classical (Ṭ, Q, IK) reads *literal* bodily transformation (Divine power unconstrained); some modernists read *metaphorical* (ape-like *character*: mimicry without understanding; swine-like *appetite*: consumption without restraint) — a reading classical exegetes generally reject for these texts while affirming *moral* similitudes elsewhere. *The abuse-guard*: the verse targets *specific historical violators*, never licenses epithets against Jews generally — the Prophet ﷺ forbade abusing the transformed ("God never gives transformed ones offspring" — Muslim 2663, severing lineage-linkage); hurling "apes and swine" at Jews today violates the verse's specificity, the Prophet's ﷺ restraint, and 60:8's justice. Cite the warning; never weaponize the wording. *Maskh* warns violators; it arms no abusers.

**Ṭāghūt: The False Gods Catalogued**

*ʿAbada'ṭ-ṭāghūt* — "worshippers of *ṭāghūt*" (*ṭāghūt*: false god, idol, tyrant, rebel — from *ṭughyān*, exceeding bounds): the verse's final specimen-category, and the Qur'an's *ṭāghūt*-theology needs its catalogue. *Qur'anic ṭawāghīt*: idols (16:36: *"avoid ṭāghūt"*), Satan (4:76: *"friends of Satan... ṭāghūt's party"*), tyrannical judges (4:60: seeking judgment from *ṭāghūt* — Jibt-and-Ṭāghūt, 4:51), Pharaoh (79:17: *"he transgressed"* — *ṭaghā*), wealth-and-power idols (18:32–43's garden-boaster). *The defining trait*: *exceeding bounds* — claiming worship, obedience, or legislation belonging to God. *The disavowal-command*: *"whoever disbelieves in ṭāghūt, believing in God, has grasped the firmest handhold"* (2:256) — *kufr-bi'ṭ-ṭāghūt* preceding *īmān-bi'Llāh* (negation before affirmation, as the *shahādah*'s *lā ilāha* precedes *illa'Llāh*). *The verse's charge*: some of the resented-history's specimens *worshipped ṭāghūt* — calf-worship (2:51, 92–93), Baal-consulting, tyrant-obeying. The mirror's edge: mockers of comprehensive belief (v. 59) descend from *ṭāghūt*-worshippers — resentment's genealogy exposed. Disbelieve every *ṭāghūt* (idols, tyrants, caprices enthroned); believe the One God — the handhold firm, the station best, the path even.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:61

> **And when they come unto you, they say, "We believe," but they enter unbelieving and leave unbelieving. And God knows best what they conceal.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Entering and Leaving Unbelieving: The Commuters of Hypocrisy**

*When they come unto you, they say "we believe," but they enter unbelieving* (*wa qad dakhalū bi'l-kufr*) *and leave unbelieving* (*wa qad kharajū bih*) — hypocrites (or hostile Jews feigning goodwill (Ṭ)) commuting between camps. The verse's symmetry is devastating: entrance and exit identical — the visit changed nothing. True encounters with truth transform; theirs were tourism. *God knows best what they conceal* (*yaktumūn*) — the concealed being hostility, mockery, and intelligence-gathering (cf. v. 41's listeners). The verse teaches believers a painful discernment: professions of fellowship must be weighed against trajectories — do visitors arrive open and depart changed, or arrive spying and depart sneering? Communities must welcome seekers while recognizing scouts. Hospitality without naivety: receive all, trust the transformed, note the unchanged.

**Dakhalū/Kharajū: The Unchanged Commute**

*Dakhalū bi'l-kufr, kharajū bihi* — "they entered with disbelief, exited with it": the commute's *sameness* is the indictment — and transformation's absence needs its counter-portraits, for genuine encounters with truth *change* entrants. *Changed entrants*: ʿUmar entered Khabbāb's house murderous, exited Muslim (the *sīrah*'s most dramatic commute); the Najāshī's envoys entered curious, exited weeping (vv. 82–83); Abū Sufyān entered conquered, exited converted (conquest-night); the Jinn entered listening, exited warning (72:1–2; 46:29–32). *Unchanged entrants*: Quraysh's negotiators (ʿUtbah entered debating, exited dazed-but-denying), Khaybar's coaches (v. 41 — coached concealment, unchanged), these commuters (entered denying, exited denying). *The diagnostic*: encounters with truth are *reactors* — the open transform (heat, light, change), the sealed transit (inert throughput). *God knows best what they conceal* (*yaktumūn* — kitmān: active concealment, cf. 2:42, 140, 159, 174) — the concealed cargo: hostility (plotting), mockery (v. 57–58), espionage (v. 41's listening). Communities need the verse's X-ray: welcome entrants (hospitality commanded), observe commutes (transformation hoped), note cargo (concealment detected). Receive all; trust the transformed; guard against the transiting.

**Kitmān: Concealment's Forms and Wages**

*Yaktumūn* (they conceal) — *kitmān* (active concealment) — the verse's closing charge, and its Qur'anic dossier needs compiling, for concealment is hypocrisy's *craft*. *Concealed objects*: scriptural truths (2:42: *"conceal not truth knowingly"*; 2:159–160: concealing clear proofs — *cursed by God and cursers*; 3:71, 187: the covenant *"make it clear, conceal it not"* — betrayed), hostility (this verse; 3:118: *"hatred shows from mouths; breasts conceal greater"*), belief (the secret believer — 40:28 — *praiseworthy* concealment under persecution, the exception proving the rule). *Concealment's agents*: scriptured concealers (2:140, 146: knowing truth *"as they know their sons"* yet hiding), hypocrite concealers (this verse; 47:29–30's hidden rancors), pagan concealers (16:23). *Wages*: curse (2:159, 161), exposure (47:29–30: *"God will bring forth... We would show them"*), Fire. *The believer's counter-craft*: *iẓhār* (manifestation) — truth spoken (3:187's covenant kept), testimony borne (2:283: *"conceal not testimony"*), clarity offered (14:4's plain speech). *God knows best* (*aʿlamu*) — concealment's futility sealed: hidden from hosts, naked to Heaven. Conceal never (except the persecuted believer's *taqiyyah* — 16:106's licensed exception); manifest always. The sealed breast is Heaven's open book.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:62

> **And thou seest many of them hastening towards sin and enmity, and their consuming what is unlawful. Evil indeed is that which they do!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Hastening Toward Sin: Velocity as Verdict**

*Thou seest many of them hastening* (*yusāriʿūn* — racing, cf. vv. 41, 52) *toward sin* (*ithm*) *and enmity* (*ʿudwān* — aggression, transgression) *and consuming the unlawful* (*suḥt*, cf. v. 42). Three destinations, one velocity: *racing*. Speed reveals desire — the reluctant dawdle toward duty, the eager sprint toward indulgence. The verse's *seeing* (*tarā* — "thou seest") makes the Prophet ﷺ (and reader) a witness: this is observable conduct, not imputed motive. *Evil indeed is what they do* (*la-bi'sa mā kānū yaʿmalūn*) — the Qur'an's verdict-formula (*bi'sa*: how evil — cf. 2:90; 3:187; 5:63, 79). The trinity of *ithm–ʿudwān–suḥt* maps corruption's anatomy: *ithm* (sin against God), *ʿudwān* (aggression against people), *suḥt* (illicit gain as livelihood). Where the three converge and *hasten*, a community is not merely sinning but *sprinting* — velocity as verdict, haste as confession.

**Ithm and ʿUdwān: Sin Versus Aggression**

*Ithm* (sin) *and ʿudwān* (enmity-aggression) — the pair recurs as cooperation's *forbidden* object (*"cooperate not in ithm and ʿudwān,"* v. 2) — and the distinction structures all moral analysis. *Ithm* (from *a-th-m*: slowness, lagging — sin as *lagging* behind good): offenses against *God* primarily — disbelief, hypocrisy, ritual neglect, private vice — the vertical breach. *ʿUdwān* (from *ʿ-d-w*: crossing, exceeding — aggression as *boundary-crossing*): offenses against *creatures* primarily — murder, theft, slander, oppression — the horizontal breach. *Their relation*: *ithm* often *breeds* ʿudwān (God-disregard licensing man-harm); *ʿudwān* always *includes* ithm (man-harm defying God). *The verse's order* (ithm → ʿudwān → suḥt): vertical breach, horizontal breach, livelihood-of-breach — corruption's *descent*: God abandoned, men attacked, sin monetized. *The racing* (*musāraʿah*): velocity toward all three — eagerness diagnosing love (the hastener *loves* the destination). Contrast the *other* racing: *sāriʿū ilā maghfirah* (3:133: race toward *forgiveness*), *istabiqū'l-khayrāt* (v. 48: race in *goods*) — velocity's *redemption*: the same energy, reversed direction. Two races, opposite finishes: hasten toward sin (this verse's losers) or toward forgiveness (3:133's winners). Velocity confesses; direction decides.

**"Evil Indeed": Bi'sa as Qur'anic Verdict**

*La-bi'sa mā kānū yaʿmalūn* — "how evil is what they did": *bi'sa* (evil, wretched) — the Qur'an's verdict-exclamation — and its usages form a *condemnation-liturgy* worth hearing whole. *Bi'sa*-verdicted: the disbelievers' purchase (2:90: *"evil is what they sold themselves for"*), concealers' bargain (3:187: *"evil is what they purchase"*), the hypocrites' works (this verse; v. 63), hellfire's resting (2:126; 3:12, 162; 8:16 — *bi'sa'l-maṣīr*, evil destination), the wrongdoers' exchange (18:50: *"evil is the exchange for wrongdoers"*), names-of-mockery (49:11: *"evil is a name: iniquity after belief"*), the arrogant's abode (16:29; 39:72: *bi'sa mathwā'l-mutakabbirīn*). *The formula's force*: *bi'sa* is *judicial* exclamation — the Judge's "guilty," pronounced mid-trial, verdict-before-sentence. *Its antonym*: *niʿma* (how excellent — 3:173: *"excellent is the Trustee"*; 4:58: *"excellent is His instruction"*) — the liturgy's praise-pole. The believer internalizes both: *bi'sa*-reflex toward sin (instinctive revulsion), *niʿma*-reflex toward good (instinctive delight). *Evil indeed what they do* — the verse trains the reflex on hastening-corruption. Cultivate it: see sin-racing, feel *bi'sa*; see good-racing, feel *niʿma*. Verdicts rehearsed now; sentences pronounced then.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:63

> **Why do not the sages and the rabbis forbid them from uttering sin and consuming what is unlawful? Evil indeed is that which they do!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Silence of Scholars: When Sages Won't Forbid**

*Why do not the rabbāniyyūn* (sages, cf. v. 44) *and aḥbār* (rabbis) *forbid them* (*law lā yanhāhum* — "if only they would forbid") *from uttering sin* (*qawl al-ithm* — sinful speech: lies, distortions, mockery) *and consuming the unlawful* (*suḥt*)? The verse pivots condemnation from sinners to *silent scholars* — and the pivot's severity is deliberate: the scholars' *omission* earns the same *bi'sa* (how evil) as the sinners' *commission*. Al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī reportedly called this the Qur'an's sternest verse against scholars. The logic: sinners sin from appetite; scholars' silence sins from calculation — fear (v. 44's *fear not men*), purchase (v. 44's *paltry price*), or comfort. A community where scholars forbid evil retains its immune system; where they fall silent, corruption metastasizes unchecked. The verse is the Qur'an's charter for the duty of *nahy ʿan al-munkar* (forbidding wrong) — addressed first to the learned, then to all (3:104, 110; 9:71).

**Law Lā: The Reproach That Shames Silence**

*Law lā yanhāhum* — "why do they not forbid?" (*law lā*: if-only-not — reproach-particle): the grammar *itself* shames — and the Qur'an's *law-lā*-reproaches deserve hearing as a chorus. *Reproached silences*: scholars not forbidding (this verse), believers not thinking-well (24:12: *"why did they not think good?"* — the slander-test), witnesses not coming (24:13: *"why did they not bring witnesses?"*), the able not striving (47:20's wish for a sūrah, then disliked fighting). *The particle's psychology*: *law lā* expresses *disappointed expectation* — the reproached *should* have acted (capacity present, duty clear), yet didn't. Scholars *could* forbid (knowledge possessed, pulpits held) and *should* (covenant taken — 3:187; office defined — v. 44) — yet silence. *The shame's function*: *law lā* doesn't *punish* (no sentence pronounced) but *exposes* (conscience activated) — reproach as reform-tool. Every silent scholar hearing it stands self-tried: knowledge? possessed. Platform? held. Forbidding? absent. *Law lā* — why not? The only answers are fear (v. 44 forbids), price (v. 44 forbids), or comfort (no verse permits). Shame unto reform: speak now; the *law-lā* lifts when lips move.

**Nahy ʿan al-Munkar: The Duty's Levels and Limits**

The verse charters *nahy ʿan al-munkar* (forbidding wrong) — and the duty's full jurisprudence needs stating, for silence and vigilantism both violate it. *The levels* (Muslim 49): *"whoever sees wrong, let him change it with his hand; if unable, with his tongue; if unable, with his heart — and that is the weakest faith."* *Hand* (authority's level: rulers, parents, managers — coercive correction within jurisdiction). *Tongue* (scholars' and citizens' level: counsel, critique, preaching — this verse's demanded minimum for the learned). *Heart* (everyone's minimum: inward rejection — hating wrong, refusing normalization). *The conditions* (al-Nawawī, al-Ghazālī): knowledge (the wrong *is* wrong — no forbidding in legitimate differences), gentleness (counsel, not cruelty — *"invite with wisdom and good instruction,"* 16:125), efficacy-prospect (no forbidding where harm exceeds benefit — greater wrongs provoked), privacy (private sins privately counseled; public sins publicly addressed). *The scholars' burden* (this verse): tongue-level *obligatory* on the learned — silence inexcusable where speech is safe; where unsafe, heart-level minimum with wisdom-calibrated speech. *Bi'sa mā kānū yaṣnaʿūn* (evil what they *craft* — *ṣunʿ*: deliberate making — silence as *manufactured* omission). Forbid by hand if empowered, tongue if learned, heart always. Never craft silence.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:64

> **And the Jews say, "God's Hand is fettered." Fettered are their hands, and they are cursed for what they say. Nay, His two Hands are outspread; He expends howsoever He will. And that which has been sent down unto thee from thy Lord will increase many of them in rebellion and disbelief. So grieve not over disbelieving people.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"God's Hand Is Fettered": The Blasphemy of Divine Stinginess**

*The Jews say: God's Hand is fettered* (*maghlūlah* — chained, tied) — meaning, per the reports: God is stingy, withholding bounty (famine-era taunt (Ṭ)), or His Hand tied from punishing (defiance), or from giving after creation's completion (deistic miserliness). *Fettered are their hands* (*ghullat aydīhim*) — the curse returned in kind: stinginess rebounding on the accusers (manifested in proverbial miserliness or in eschatological fettering, 69:30) — *and they are cursed* (*luʿinū*) *for what they say*. *Nay, His two Hands are outspread* (*mabsūṭatān*) — *He expends howsoever He will* (*yunfiqu kayfa yashā'*). The verse's economics: Divine giving is neither exhausted nor constrained — *"He gives whom He will without reckoning"* (2:212; 3:27, 37; 24:38). Famine, poverty, and withholding are *distributions* of wisdom, never *constraints* of poverty — God withholds as He gives: *kayfa yashā'* (howsoever He wills). To call the All-Generous stingy is the precise inversion of reality — like calling the sun dark during an eclipse of one's own making.

**Yad Allāh: The Hand of God in Theology**

*Yad Allāh* (God's Hand) — *fettered* (their claim), *outspread* (His rebuttal), *two* (His self-description) — the anthropomorphic expression needs theology's careful handling, for the verse is a *ṣifāt*-text (Divine-attributes). *The affirmation* (Sunnī mainstream): God's Hand is *real* (*thābitah* — affirmed as He affirmed: 38:75's *"what I created with My two Hands"*; 48:10's *"God's Hand above their hands"*; 57:29's bounty in Hand), *unlike* creation (*bilā kayf* — without modality: *"nothing is like Him,"* 42:11), *un-emptied* (*bilā taʿṭīl* — not voided into metaphor-only). *The interpretations*: *mabsūṭatān* (outspread) signifying generosity's perfection (all schools agree the *meaning-context* is bounty); *maghlūlah* (fettered) signifying their stinginess-charge. *The schools' accents*: Atharīs affirming Hand literally-without-likeness; Ashʿarīs affirming with *tafwīḍ* (modality consigned) or *ta'wīl* (power/nbounty interpretations); Muʿtazilīs metaphorizing fully. *The verse's own balance*: *two Hands* (affirmation — duality resisting pure-metaphor), *outspread* (generosity — meaning accessible), *yunfiqu kayfa yashā'* (will — distribution wise). The blasphemy-verse thus doubles as attribute-creed: affirm the Hands, deny the fetters, ponder the outspreading. Hands real, fetters void, bounty boundless — *bilā kayf*, with full conviction.

**Revelation That Increases Rebellion: The Hardening Effect**

*What was sent down to thee from thy Lord will increase many of them in rebellion* (*ṭughyān*) *and disbelief* — revelation as *hardener* of the resistant (cf. 17:41, 60, 82; v. 68). The mechanism is not in the revelation but the recipient: the same rain grows wheat or thorns depending on soil (14:24–26); the same Book heals believers and hardens deniers (17:82: *"healing and mercy for believers, loss for wrongdoers"*). Each new verse confronts the resistant with a choice — submit or resist — and each resistance deposits another layer of seal (2:7; 4:155; 16:108). *So grieve not over disbelieving people* (*lā ta'sa ʿalā'l-qawmi'l-kāfirīn*; cf. v. 41, 68) — the warner's consolation repeated: their hardening is their harvest; your grief cannot irrigate sealed soil. Deliver, pity from proper distance, and release outcomes to the Outspread Hands.

**Ṭughyān-Ziyādah: How Guidance Hardens Resisters**

*La-yazīdanna kathīran minhum... ṭughyānan wa kufran* — "it will surely increase many in rebellion and disbelief": revelation *increasing* misguidance — the paradox needs its psychology, for it troubles every warner. *The mechanism* (recipient-side): each revelation-presentation forces *decision* (submit-or-resist); each *resistance* deposits seal-layer (2:7's *khatm*, 4:155's *ṭabʿ*, 16:108's covering); accumulated seals *harden* (5:13's *qaswah*; 39:22's woe-to-hardened); hardened hearts *provoke* further revelation-rejection (17:41's *"it increases them only in aversion"*). *The rain-parable* (14:24–26; 17:82): same water, wheat-or-thorns — soil deciding; same Book, healing-or-loss — heart deciding. *The warner's consolation* (*lā ta'sa* — grieve not): hardening is *their* harvest (choices compounded), not *your* failure (delivery completed) — Noah's 950 years (29:14) hardening his people yet perfecting his mission. *The pastoral caution*: warners mustn't *accelerate* hardening (harshness sealing, 3:159's *"had you been harsh, they would have dispersed"*) nor *fear* it (grief bounded, v. 41). Present clearly, gently, persistently — then release. The Outspread Hands distribute: guidance to openers, hardening to closers. *Kayfa yashā'* — howsoever He wills, wisely always.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:65

> **And had the People of the Book believed and been reverent, We would have absolved them of their evil deeds, and We would have caused them to enter the Gardens of bliss.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Open Door: What Belief Would Have Won Them**

*Had the People of the Book believed* (*āmanū*) *and been reverent* (*ittaqaw*) — *We would have absolved their evil deeds* (*kaffarnā sayyi'ātihim*) *and entered them into Gardens of bliss* (*naʿīm*). The verse holds the door open mid-polemic: condemnation of specific corruptions never cancels the universal invitation. Two conditions, two promises: *belief* (in God, His messengers including Muḥammad ﷺ, and the Last Day) plus *reverence* (piety in deed) earn *absolution* (past covered) plus *Paradise* (future secured). The formula matches Islam's universal offer (4:122; 29:7; 39:53) — no communal surcharge, no ethnic discount. Polemic in the Qur'an is never pogrom's prelude; it is diagnosis preceding prescription. The physician's harshest words about the disease accompany the gentlest insistence that cure remains available. Every hard verse in this section (vv. 51–64) is relativized by this one: the door stands open, the terms are clear, the welcome is warm.

**Law Āmanū: The Conditional That Condemns No One Finally**

*Wa law anna... āmanū wa'ttaqaw* — "had they believed and been reverent": the counterfactual-conditional (*law*) — and its *pastoral* function needs stress: it condemns *states*, never *persons-finally*. *The conditional's logic*: *had* they believed (they haven't-yet) → absolution-and-Gardens (still-available). The door's openness is *structural*: condition stated (belief-plus-reverence), promise attached (absolution-plus-Paradise), addressees living (capable still). *Qur'anic parallels*: Pharaoh invited till drowning (20:43–44's gentle speech; 10:90's too-late belief marking the *only* closure-point — death); Quraysh warned yet welcomed (conquest-amnesty); hypocrites invited to 4:146's cure. *Polemic relativized*: every hard verse (vv. 51–64: alliance-bans, curse-texts, *maskh*-warnings) operates *within* this conditional's shelter — condemnations of *persisting* states, invitations to *abandoning* them. *Takfīr*-restraint follows: no living person's final verdict is pronounceable — the *law-āmanū* door stands till death's *ghar­gharah* (throat-rattle — al-Tirmidhī 3537). Diagnose states boldly; damn persons never finally. The conditional condemns trajectories, invites travelers. Open door, clear terms, warm welcome — till the last breath.

**Takfīr al-Sayyi'āt: Absolution's Mechanics**

*La-kaffarnā ʿanhum sayyi'ātihim* — "We would surely absolve their evil deeds": *takfīr* (covering, expiation) of *sayyi'āt* (evil deeds) — and absolution's *mechanics* need exhibiting, for the promise's richness exceeds casual reading. *Absolution's agents* (Qur'anic inventory): belief-plus-reverence (this verse), avoiding major sins (4:31: *"if you avoid great sins, We absolve your misdeeds"*), migrating-and-striving (3:195), charity (2:271: *"charity absolves some evil deeds"*), good deeds (11:114: *"good deeds remove evil ones"*), afflictions (Bukhārī 5640: fever/thorn expiating), rituals (prayers, Ramadan, Ḥajj — Muslim 233, 1349). *Absolution's scope*: *sayyi'āt* (misdeeds) covered — pasts *erased* (25:70: even *converted* to good deeds for repentant believers), records *cleansed*, shame *lifted*. *This verse's absolution*: retroactive universality — *all* prior evil deeds (denials, distortions, mockery — vv. 41–64's catalogue) covered by belief-plus-reverence. *The Gardens* (*jannāt al-naʿīm* — Gardens of Bliss): absolution's *positive* wage — not merely hell-avoided but bliss-entered. The offer's extravagance: lifetimes of opposition exchanged for eternity of bliss — two conditions met, infinite promise kept. Absolution mechanical (conditions trigger promise), mercy personal (the Absolver delights — 39:53). Believe, revere — absolved, admitted.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:66

> **And had they observed the Torah and the Gospel, and what was sent down unto them from their Lord, they would have eaten from above them and from below their feet. Among them is a moderate community. But many of them — evil is what they do!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Observing the Books: The Prosperity of Fidelity**

*Had they observed* (*aqāmū* — established, upheld) *the Torah, the Gospel, and what was sent down from their Lord* (the Qur'an (Ṭ, Z), or all revelations) — *they would have eaten from above them and from below their feet*: heavenly rains and earthly produce (Ṭ, Z) — prosperity as fidelity's fruit (cf. 7:96: *had towns believed, We would have opened blessings of heaven and earth*; 20:124's straitened life for the averter). The verse states revelation's *worldly* dividend: societies ordered by Divine guidance flourish materially — not by magic but by mechanism: trust reduces transaction costs, charity circulates wealth, justice secures investment, temperance preserves health. *Among them is a moderate community* (*ummah muqtaṣidah* — balanced, temperate; cf. 3:113–114; 7:159, 168; 28:52–53) — the Qur'an's fairness: condemnation never blankets; a righteous remnant is always acknowledged. *But many of them — evil is what they do* (*sā'a mā yaʿmalūn*) — the majority-verdict matching verse 62–63's *bi'sa*. Fidelity feeds; infidelity starves — above, below, and within.

**Aqāmū: Establishment as More Than Belief**

*Law annahum aqāmū* — "had they *established*" (*iqāmah*: erecting, upholding — the verb of prayer-establishment): observance as *erection*, not mere assent — and the distinction carries the verse's demand. *Belief* (*īmān*): heart's assent — necessary, insufficient alone. *Establishment* (*iqāmah*): raising revelation into *structures* — courts judging by it (vv. 43–49), markets ordered by it (no *ribā*, no *gharar*), households governed by it, pulpits proclaiming it. *The Torah-Gospel-plus*: *mā unzila ilayhim min rabbihim* (what was sent from their Lord) — the Qur'an (Ṭ, Z: addressed to contemporaries) or all-revelation (universal reading): establishment's *full* curriculum — own-scripture honored, final-revelation embraced. *The dividend* (*akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim* — eating from above-and-below): 7:96's opened-blessings, Noah's rain-and-wealth promise (71:10–12: *"seek forgiveness — He sends rain, supplies wealth-and-children"*), Sharīʿah-societies' historical prosperities. *The mechanism* (non-magical): trust (contracts honored), circulation (hoarding banned, charity mandated), security (justice deterring predation), sustainability (waste *isrāf*-banned, balance *mīzān*-commanded). Establish revelation structurally; prosperity follows systemically. Assent alone starves; establishment feeds — above, below, within.

**Ummah Muqtaṣidah: The Moderate Remnant**

*Minhum ummatun muqtaṣidah* — "among them is a moderate community" (*qaṣd*: middle-ness, temperance, straightness): the Qur'an's remnant-acknowledgment — and its fairness-theology needs celebrating, for polemic never blankets. *The remnant-texts*: 3:113–114 (*"not all alike: an upright community reciting, prostrating, believing"*), 7:159 (Moses' people guiding by truth), 7:168 (*"among them righteous, among them otherwise"*), 28:52–53 (scriptured believers: *"we were Muslims before it"*), this verse. *Iqtiṣād* (moderation): neither *ghulūw* (excess — deifying Jesus, 4:171; 5:77) nor *tafrīṭ* (neglect — abandoning law, v. 68's non-observance) — the middle path *within* each community. *The fairness-principle*: condemnation *specifies* (vv. 41–64's named behaviors), praise *particularizes* (remnants named) — collective guilt never assigned, collective innocence never assumed. *The apologetic fruit*: Muslims cannot stereotype (remnants acknowledged), cannot despair of communities (moderates present), must *seek* the righteous across lines (dialogue-partners identified). *Bi'sa*-majorities (v. 63) coexist with *muqtaṣid*-minorities — majorities verdicting, minorities witnessing. Acknowledge remnants; ally with moderates; invite majorities. Fairness universal; hope particular; invitation perpetual.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:67

> **O Messenger! Proclaim that which has been sent down unto thee from thy Lord. If thou doest it not, then thou hast not conveyed His Message. And God will protect thee from mankind. Truly God guides not disbelieving people.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Total Proclamation: The Messenger's Non-Negotiable Mandate**

*O Messenger* (*yā ayyuhā'r-rasūl* — the office-title, used only here and 5:41's neighborhood in direct address) — *proclaim* (*balligh* — deliver fully, convey completely) *what was sent down from thy Lord*. *If thou doest it not, thou hast not conveyed His Message* (*risālah*) — partial delivery equals non-delivery: the mandate is total or void. The verse descended, per reports, commanding fearless proclamation — the Prophet ﷺ had hesitated over verses offending powerful rejecters (Q, Ṭ) — and after it he dismissed his guards: *"God protects me"* (Q, Ṭ). *God will protect thee* (*yaʿṣimuka*) *from mankind* — *ʿiṣmah* (protection, preservation): assassination-attempts foiled (the poisoned sheep — Khaybar; the rock-drop — Banū Naḍīr; the strangling — ʿUqbah at the Kaʿbah), plots exposed (9:74's Tabūk-ambush), until the mission completed. *God guides not disbelieving people* — plots against revelation cannot ultimately prosper. The verse founds every warner's courage: proclaim *all*, fear *none*, trust *the Protector*. Guards dismissed; God sufficient.

**Balligh: Conveyance as the Prophetic Office**

*Balligh* (proclaim, convey fully) — from *balāgh* (complete delivery): the prophetic office's *essence* — and its theology needs full statement. *The office defined*: *"the Messenger's duty is only clear conveyance"* (5:92, 99; 16:35, 82; 24:54; 29:18; 64:12 — the refrain repeated sixfold). *Totality demanded* (this verse): partial-conveyance = non-conveyance — no verse suppressed for fear, favor, or strategy. *The Prophet's ﷺ completion*: *"have I conveyed?"* (Farewell Sermon — *"Allāhumma'h-shhad,"* O God, witness — al-Bukhārī 67; Muslim 1679; Abū Dāwūd 1948) — the mission's seal, witnesses charged (*"let the present convey to the absent"*). *The Companions' continuation*: *tablīgh* inherited — Muʿādh to Yemen (*"convey from me though one verse"* — al-Bukhārī 3461), the dispersing Companions carrying verses to horizons. *Fear's veto* (this verse's context): hesitation over offending-powerful *overruled* — proclamation's completeness outranking prudence's cautions. Every warner inherits *balligh*: convey *all* (no topical trimming), convey *clearly* (no obscurantism), convey *fearlessly* (protection promised). Partial pulpits preach partial messages; the verse voids them. *Balligh mā unzila* — all of it, all of them, all the time.

**ʿIṣmah: Divine Protection of the Mission**

*Wa'Llāhu yaʿṣimuka mina'n-nās* — "God will protect thee from mankind": *ʿiṣmah* (protection, preservation, immunity) — and its *sīrah*-ledger proves the promise historically. *Foiled attempts*: strangling at the Kaʿbah (ʿUqbah ibn Abī Muʿayṭ's cloak-twist — Abū Bakr intervening, *"would you kill a man for saying: my Lord is God?"* — al-Bukhārī 3678); the Naḍīr rock-drop (Gabriel's warning, the Prophet ﷺ rising mid-siege — Ibn Hishām); Khaybar's poisoned sheep (Zaynab's leg confessing — *"this bone informs me it is poisoned"* — Abū Dāwūd 4512); the Hijrah pursuit (Suraqah's horse swallowed thrice — al-Bukhārī 3906); Tabūk's ravine-ambush (9:74's *"they resolved what they attained not"*); Uḥud's wounds (protected *through* injury — mission preserved, person tested). *The guards dismissed* (Q, Ṭ): protection transferred from swords to Heaven. *The scope*: mission-*ʿiṣmah* (delivery guaranteed) distinguished from person-*ibtilā'* (trials permitted — wounds, grief, hunger) — the Message shielded absolutely, the Messenger tested humanly. *The inheritance*: warners promised *mission*-protection (truth preserved through them) not *person*-immunity (martyrdom honored). Proclaim fearlessly; the Protector preserves purposes. Guards optional; God sufficient.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:68

> **Say, "O People of the Book! You stand upon nothing till you observe the Torah and the Gospel, and what was sent down unto you from your Lord." And that which has been sent down unto thee from thy Lord will increase many of them in rebellion and disbelief. So grieve not over disbelieving people.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"You Stand Upon Nothing": The Bankruptcy of Non-Observance**

*Lastum ʿalā shay'* — "you stand upon nothing" — revelation's bleakest audit: scripture-possessors who observe nothing possess nothing. *Till you observe* (*tuqīmū* — establish, cf. v. 66's *aqāmū*) *the Torah, the Gospel, and what was sent from your Lord* (the Qur'an). The verse's occasion sharpens it: Jews demanding the Prophet ﷺ affirm *all* of the Torah's present claims, Christians demanding affirmation of Jesus' divinity — revelation answers: affirmation without observance is bankruptcy (Ṭ, W). The audit generalizes across all scriptured communities including Muslims: *ḥadīth*-possessors who practice nothing, *fiqh*-masters who embody nothing, *memorizers* who live nothing — *lastum ʿalā shay'* (you stand on nothing). Possession without practice is portfolio without assets. The verse's *ḥattā* (until) holds the remedy: observe — establish — and nothing becomes everything.

**Lastum ʿalā Shay': Bankruptcy's Audit Criteria**

*Lastum ʿalā shay'* — "you are upon nothing": the audit's *criteria* need specifying, for bankruptcy is *diagnosable*. *Assets claimed*: scripture-possession (Torah, Gospel, Qur'an), lineage (Abraham's seed, chosen people, best-nation — 3:110), ritual-performance (Sabbaths, sacraments, ṣalāts), knowledge (Torah-mastery, theology-degrees, *ijāzahs*). *Liabilities exposed*: non-observance (laws unapplied — vv. 43–49), distortion (texts edited — v. 41), mockery (worship ridiculed — vv. 57–58), alliance-betrayal (truth sold — vv. 51–52). *The audit-formula*: assets minus observance = nothing — possession *without* practice voids holdings. *Islam's parallel audit*: *"the parable of those entrusted with Torah then bearing it not is as the ass carrying books"* (62:5) — knowledge-beasts, load-bearing without comprehension; *"do you enjoin righteousness, forgetting yourselves, while reciting the Book?"* (2:44); *"most hateful to God that you say what you do not"* (61:2–3). *The remedy* (*ḥattā tuqīmū* — until you *establish*): practice converts portfolio to assets — observance capitalizing knowledge. Audit yourself: possessions? many. Practices? —. The verse computes net worth. Establish; nothing becomes everything.

**The Hardening Refrain: Grief Bounded Again**

*What was sent down to thee will increase many of them in rebellion and disbelief* — the refrain from verse 64, repeated verbatim: revelation hardens resisters (see v. 64). *So grieve not over disbelieving people* — the consolation from verses 41 and 64, repeated: the warner's grief bounded (see v. 41). Repetition here is pastoral, not redundant: the Prophet ﷺ needed thrice-told comfort because rejection wounded thrice-deep — mockery (vv. 57–58), resentment (v. 59), and now bankruptcy-proclaimed (v. 68). The triple consolation structures every warner's resilience: deliver (v. 67's *balligh*), diagnose (v. 68's *lastum*), release (v. 68's *lā ta'sa*). Proclaim all, name nothingness, grieve nothing. The mission's emotional economy: expenditure on delivery, never on despair.

**Triple Consolation: The Warner's Resilience Structure**

*Lā taḥzan / lā ta'sa* (grieve not) — thrice-uttered in this sūrah (vv. 41, 64, 68) — structures the warner's *resilience*, and the triad's architecture needs mapping. *Consolation one* (v. 41): grief over *hypocrites-and-deceivers* (mouth-belief, heart-denial, verdict-shopping) — bounded by *Divine disposal* (guidance God's act, delivery yours). *Consolation two* (v. 64): grief over *blasphemers-and-hardeners* (fettered-Hand charge, revelation increasing rebellion) — bounded by *recipient-responsibility* (hardening their harvest, bounty His distribution). *Consolation three* (this verse): grief over the *bankrupt* (standing-on-nothing, observance-abandoned) — bounded by *remedy-offered* (establish-and-stand; refusal theirs). *The structure*: deliver (67) → diagnose (68a) → release (68b) — expenditure on *proclamation*, never on *despair*. *Qur'anic expansion*: 18:6 (grief *killing* — bounded), 26:3 (grief *destroying* — bounded), 35:8 (grief over the adorned-strayed — bounded: *"grieve not"*), 16:127 (grief-and-distress both bounded: *"grieve not over them, nor be distressed by their scheming"*). Resilience is *commanded*, not merely permitted — grief beyond bounds disobeys. Proclaim, diagnose, release — thrice-consoled, mission-steady.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:69

> **Truly those who believe, and those who are Jews, the Sabeans, and the Christians — whosoever believes in God and the Last Day and works righteousness — no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**No Fear Nor Grief: Salvation's Universal Formula**

*Those who believe* (Muslims), *Jews* (*hādū*), *Sabeans* (*ṣābi'ūn*), *Christians* (*naṣārā*) — *whosoever believes in God and the Last Day and works righteousness: no fear upon them, nor shall they grieve* (*lā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn*). The verse (near-identical to 2:62; cf. 22:17) states salvation's universal formula: right belief (God, Last Day) plus right action (righteousness) equals security (no fear of future, no grief over past). The Sabeans — monotheistic Gnostics of Mesopotamia (star-venerating Ḥarrānians or Baptist Mandaeans (Ṭ, Q)) — extend the formula beyond the familiar three. The verse's generosity is structural: four communities named, one criterion applied, identical promise issued. Whatever the debates about post-Qur'anic applicability (see below), the verse's plain drift is unmistakable: God's mercy refuses communal monopoly. Labels do not save; belief-and-righteousness do.

**2:62, 5:69, 22:17: The Triad Compared**

The salvation-formula recurs *thrice* (2:62; 5:69; 22:17) — and the triad's variations carry meaning worth comparing word-by-word. *2:62* (early Madinan): *"those who believed, Jews, Christians, Sabeans — whoever believes in God and Last Day, working righteousness: reward with Lord, no fear, no grief."* *5:69* (late Madinan): *"those who believe, Jews, Sabeans, Christians — whoever believes... "* (Sabeans promoted before Christians; *reward*-clause dropped, security-promise kept). *22:17* (eschatological): *"God will judge between them on Resurrection Day"* — believers, Jews, Sabeans, Christians, Magians (*majūs* added), polytheists (judgment *between*, verdicts deferred). *The trajectory*: promise (2:62) → promise-confirmed (5:69) → judgment-deferred (22:17) — generosity *stated twice*, adjudication *reserved finally*. *The Sabeans' promotion* (5:69): order-shift signaling non-hierarchy — sequence carries no ranking. *The Magians' addition* (22:17): Zoroastrians included in *judgment's* scope (verdict God's). *The polytheists' inclusion* (22:17): even they face *judgment*, not pre-verdict — God's court tries all. The triad's message: promise generously (62, 69), judge never finally (17). State the formula; defer the verdicts; leave benches to God.

**Pre- or Post-Muḥammad? The Applicability Debate**

*Who* does the formula cover *after* Muḥammad ﷺ? — the verse's most-contested question, and the positions need fair hearing. *The exclusivist reading* (IK and many): the formula covers pre-Muḥammadan communities (Moses'-era Jews, Jesus'-era Christians believing rightly) plus post-Muḥammadan converts to Islam — whoever authentically receives Muḥammad's ﷺ message must embrace it (3:85: *"whoever seeks other than Islam as religion, never accepted"*; 7:158; Muslim 153: *"no Jew or Christian hearing of me, dying unbelieving, but Fire"*). *The inclusivist reading* (some moderns, with classical seeds): the formula's plain universality (*whosoever* — unrestricted) covers righteous non-Muslims even post-Qur'an — God's mercy exceeding communal bounds (2:62's placement, 5:69's late confirmation, 22:17's deferred judgment). *The middle path* (mainstream classical synthesis): the formula states *genuine* criteria (true monotheism, Last-Day belief, righteousness) — and authentic Muḥammadan reception *itself* becomes part of "believing in God" for those reached (4:136's belief-in-messengers; 7:157's unlettered-prophet belief) — while the *unreached*, misreached, and excused (*ahl al-fatrah*, the confused, the coerced) fall under Divine justice-and-mercy (17:15: *"We punish not till sending a messenger"*). State the formula generously; apply the conditions carefully; judge individuals never. The promise is God's to keep; the verdicts are God's to pronounce.

**Lā Khawf wa Lā Ḥuzn: Security's Two Poles**

*Lā khawfun ʿalayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn* — "no fear upon them, nor shall they grieve": salvation as *two-poled security* — and the formula's psychology needs savoring, for it recurs as the Qur'an's *refrain of the saved* (2:38, 62, 112, 262, 274, 277; 3:170; 5:69; 6:48; 7:35, 49; 10:62; 43:68; 46:13). *No fear* (*lā khawf*): future secured — no dread of judgment (21:103: *"the great terror grieves them not"*), no anxiety over provision (manna-economies ended, Garden-abundance begun), no terror of loss. *No grief* (*lā ḥuzn*): past absolved — no regret over sins (covered — v. 65), no sorrow over leavings (reunions — 52:21), no mourning over earth (compensated infinitely). *The poles' completeness*: fear faces *forward* (threats ahead), grief faces *backward* (losses behind) — both nullified, the present eternally serene. *The agents*: God removing (*adhabnā* — 35:34: *"praise God who removed grief from us"*), angels announcing (41:30: *"fear not, grieve not — rejoice in Paradise"*). *This verse's gift*: the two-poled security *promised across four communities* — fearlessness and grieflessness as belief-righteousness's wage, communally unrestricted. Believe, work righteousness — future fearless, past griefless, present blissful.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:70

> **Verily We made a covenant with the Children of Israel, and We sent messengers unto them. Whensoever a messenger came unto them with that which their souls desired not, some they denied, and some they slew.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Covenant and Its Killers: Israel's Prophetic Record**

*We made a covenant* (*mīthāq*) *with the Children of Israel, and sent messengers* — covenant-plus-messengers: the entrustment's fullness (cf. 2:40, 83; 5:12–14). *Whensoever a messenger came with what their souls desired not* (*mā lā tahwā anfusuhum*) — revelation colliding with appetite — *some they denied* (*kadhdhabū* — a party, *farīqan*) *and some they slew* (*yaqtulūn* — a party; present-tense for horror's vividness). The record: Zechariah sawn (tradition), John the Baptist beheaded (tradition; cf. 3:112, 183; 4:155 — *killing prophets wrongfully*), Jesus' attempted crucifixion (4:157), and — in the accusing present — plots against Muḥammad ﷺ (cf. v. 11). The verse's psychology is universal: revelation invariably brings *some* unwelcome element (a prohibition cherished-habits violate, a command comfort resists) — and the unwelcome part tests sincerity. Communities pass by submitting where desire dissents; they fail — as here catastrophically — by silencing the messenger instead of the appetite.

**Mā Lā Tahwā: The Unwelcome Parts of Revelation**

*Mā lā tahwā anfusuhum* — "what their souls desired not": revelation's *unwelcome parts* — and the concept tests *every* community, for each revelation contains desire-defying elements. *Israel's unwelcome parts*: Sabbath-restrictions (profit resented), dietary laws (appetite curbed), prophetic rebukes (pride wounded), Jesus' easings (authority threatened), Muḥammad's finality (exclusivity ended). *Quraysh's unwelcome parts*: resurrection (indulgence threatened), equality (aristocracy leveled), abandoning idols (commerce-and-custom upended). *Muslims' unwelcome parts*: zakāh (wealth taxed), ḥudūd (impulse restrained), jihād (comfort risked), gender-rules (desires ordered), interest-ban (finance restructured). *The responses*: submission (the believer's *"we hear and obey,"* 2:285 — desire surrendered), denial (the rejecter's *"we hear and disobey,"* 2:93 — messenger silenced), murder (the violent's escalation — voice extinguished). *The verse's warning*: every community faces *its* unwelcome parts; Israel's catastrophic response (denial-then-murder) stands as history's deterrent-specimen. Audit your unwelcome parts: which rulings chafe? Submit there first — sincerity is proven at desire's dissent, never at its consent.

**Killing Prophets: History's Gravest Crime**

*Farīqan kadhdhabū wa farīqan yaqtulūn* — "a party they denied, a party they kill": prophet-killing — history's gravest crime-category — and its Qur'anic dossier needs full assembly. *The charges*: 2:61 (*"killing prophets without right"* — humiliation-and-wrath's cause), 2:87 (*"a party denying, a party killing"*), 2:91, 3:21 (*"killing prophets without right, killing justice-commanders"* — double murder: guides and reformers), 3:112, 181, 183; 4:155; 5:70. *The victims* (tradition): Zechariah (sawn in the tree), John (beheaded for Herod's grudge), Isaiah (sawn), Jesus (attempted — 4:157's *"they killed him not"*), Muḥammad (attempted repeatedly — poison, rock, ambush). *The present-tense* (*yaqtulūn* — *they kill*): horror's vividness (the crime *live*, ongoing) or continuity (each generation's plots). *The gravity's logic*: prophets are God's *embassies* — killing them is deicide-attempted (murdering the Message by murdering its bearer), guidance-abolished (light extinguished at source), covenant-shattered (the *mīthāq*'s messengers-part voided). *The modern form*: prophet-killing literal is sealed (prophethood sealed); *scholar-killing, reformer-killing, truth-killing* continue (3:21's justice-commanders; journalists, *ʿulamā'*, witnesses — silenced for unwelcome truth). Deny never; kill never; submit where desire dissents. The embassies are inviolable; their violators are history's worst.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:71

> **And they supposed that there would be no trial, so they were blind and deaf. Then God relented unto them. Then again many of them were blind and deaf. And God is the Seer of what they do.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Blind and Deaf: The Trial They Denied**

*They supposed* (*ḥasibū*) *there would be no trial* (*fitnah*) — no testing, no consequence — *so they were blind and deaf* (*ʿamū wa ṣammū*): moral blindness (truth unseen) and deafness (warnings unheard) as *consequence* of consequence-denial. *Then God relented* (*tāba'Llāhu ʿalayhim*) *unto them* — respite granted (return from Babylonian exile; prosperity restored) — *then again many of them were blind and deaf*: relapse after mercy, the chronic rhythm (cf. Judges' cycle; 7:168's testing with good-and-evil). *God is the Seer* (*Baṣīr*) *of what they do* — the refrain's comfort and threat: nothing unseen, whether relapse or repentance. The verse compresses Israel's whole history into one sentence: presumption → blindness → mercy → relapse. It compresses every sinner's biography likewise: "no consequences" → numbness → respite → relapse. The cycle breaks only where presumption dies: consequences are certain, mercy is available, relapse is optional.

**Ḥasibū: Presumption as Blindness's Cause**

*Wa ḥasibū allā takūna fitnah* — "they supposed no trial would be": *ḥus­bān* (supposition, miscalculation) as *blindness's cause* — and presumption's psychology needs dissecting, for it blinds every age. *The supposition's content*: no *fitnah* (trial, testing, consequence) — impunity presumed: sins unpunished, covenants unenforced, prophets killable-without-cost. *The supposition's grounds*: past impunity (delayed punishment mistaken for canceled), present prosperity (blessings mistaken for approval — *istidrāj*, gradual luring, 7:182–183), tribal assurance (*"we are God's beloved,"* v. 18 — election mistaken for exemption). *The blindness-mechanism*: presumption *anesthetizes* (consequences unfelt), anesthesia *blinds* (warnings unseen), blindness *deafens* (rebukes unheard) — *ʿamū wa ṣammū* as presumption's wages. *Qur'anic parallels*: Quraysh's *"had we been guided..."* (39:57–59's too-late wishing), the garden-owners' *"no poor shall enter"* (68:17–33's presumption-then-ruin), Qārūn's *"given for my knowledge"* (28:78's merit-presumption). *The cure*: presumption's inversion — *suppose* trial certain (accountability assumed), *see* warnings (signs heeded), *hear* rebukes (counsel welcomed). Presumption blinds; accountability restores sight. Suppose consequences; see clearly.

**The Cycle: Presumption, Blindness, Mercy, Relapse**

*ʿAmū-ṣammū → tāba ʿalayhim → ʿamū-ṣammū* — blind-deaf, relented-upon, blind-deaf-again: Israel's *cycle* — and its universality needs charting, for nations and souls both revolve it. *Israel's revolutions*: Egypt-oppression → Exodus-mercy → calf-relapse; wilderness-murmuring → manna-mercy → rebellion-relapse; Canaan-conquest → judges-mercies → idolatry-relapses; exile → return-mercy → Second-Temple corruption-relapse. *The ummah's revolutions*: jāhiliyyah → Islam-mercy → Umayyad-luxury-relapse; Crusader-threat → Ṣalāḥuddīn-mercy → Mamlūk-faction-relapse; colonial-humiliation → independence-mercies → tyranny-relapses. *The soul's revolutions*: sin → guilt → repentance-mercy → relapse; Ramadan-elevation → Shawwāl-relapse; affliction-softening → prosperity-hardening. *The cycle's breaker*: only *presumption's death* (v. 71a's *ḥus­bān* abandoned) — consequences held certain, mercy sought perpetually, vigilance maintained post-repentance. *Wa'Llāhu Baṣīrun bi-mā yaʿmalūn* (God sees all): the cycle *witnessed* — every revolution recorded, every breaking honored. Revolve knowingly or break deliberately. The Seer sees both; mercy favors breakers.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:72

> **Certainly they disbelieve, those who say, "Truly God is the Messiah, son of Mary." Say, "Who has any power against God, if He desires to destroy the Messiah, son of Mary, his mother, and all those who are upon the earth?" Unto God belongs sovereignty over the heavens and the earth and whatsoever is between them. He creates what He wills. And God is Powerful over all things.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"God Is the Messiah": The Verdict and Its Argument**

*Certainly they disbelieve* (*laqad kafara*) *who say: God is the Messiah, son of Mary* — the verdict on Divine-incarnation claims (cf. v. 17; v. 73's Trinity-verse; 4:171–172; 9:30–31; 19:88–93). The rebuttal is ontological: *who has power against God if He desired to destroy the Messiah, his mother, and all on earth?* Contingency refutes divinity — whoever *can* be destroyed *cannot* be God; need (Mary's and Jesus' human needs — food, sleep, shelter, v. 75) refutes self-sufficiency. *Unto God belongs sovereignty* (*mulk*) *over heavens, earth, and between* — ownership universal, divinity exclusive. *He creates what He wills* (*yakhluqu mā yashā'*) — Jesus' fatherless birth is *creation's* marvel (like Adam's — 3:59), not *incarnation's* proof. *Powerful over all things* — including creating without fathers and judging without partners. The verse argues, not merely anathematizes: Christians are answered with reasons — contingency, need, sovereignty, creation — before verdicts. Polemic with proofs; conviction before condemnation.

**Laqad Kafara: The Verdict's Precision and Bounds**

*Laqad kafara'lladhīna qālū* — "certainly disbelieved those saying...": the verdict's *precision* needs bounding, for *takfīr*-recklessness and truth-timidity both abuse it. *What is verdicts*: the *proposition* (God-is-Messiah) as *kufr* (disbelief) — revelation's plain classification (vv. 17, 72, 73; 4:171; 9:30). *What is not verdicts*: individual Christians' *final destinies* (judged by God with full knowledge — 22:17's deferred tribunal; 17:15's reachedness-condition), Christians' *worldly treatment* (justice-and-kindness commanded — 60:8; v. 5's fellowship), Christians' *sincerity* (many worshipping devotedly per erring theology — 3:113–114's upright remnant). *The distinction* (proposition-verdict vs. person-verdict): Islam judges *doctrines* definitively (revelation's right) while deferring *persons* finally (God's prerogative) — condemning *shirk* absolutely, condemning *mushriks* individually never-finally (their repentance possible, their excuse assessable-only-by-God). *The application*: state the verdict on incarnation-theology plainly (timidity betrays truth), withhold final verdicts on Christian persons entirely (recklessness usurps God), treat Christians justly always (60:8 binds). Precision in doctrine, restraint in damnation, justice in dealing — the verse's verdict bounded rightly.

**Contingency Refutes Divinity: The Qur'an's Christological Argument**

*Man yamliku mina'Llāhi shay'an in arāda an yuhlika'l-Masīḥa... wa ummahū... wa man fi'l-arḍi jamīʿan* — "who possesses aught against God, if He willed destroying the Messiah, his mother, and all on earth?": the *contingency-argument* — and its philosophical fullness needs unfolding. *Premise one*: Jesus and Mary are *destructible* (mortal, needy — eating food, v. 75; sleeping, suffering, dying). *Premise two*: God is *indestructible* (Living-undying — 25:58; Self-subsisting — 2:255; death-impossible). *Conclusion*: the destructible cannot be the Indestructible — contingency and divinity are mutually exclusive. *Corollaries*: need refutes self-sufficiency (the hungry is no God — v. 75's *kānā ya'kulāni'ṭ-ṭaʿām*); birth refutes eternity (the born began — Mary's son *began*); death-intent refutes omnipotence (the killable is no Almighty). *Christian responses* (two-natures theology: Divine nature indestructible, human destructible): the Qur'an's counter — *tawḥīd*'s simplicity (One God, undivided, unpartnered — 112:1–4; composition itself contingency-implying). *Yakhluqu mā yashā'* (creates what He wills): the *alternative* explanation — virginal birth as *creative* act (Adam without parents, Eve without mother, Jesus without father — 3:59's parallel), marvel proving *God's* power, not *Jesus'* divinity. Argue ontologically; conclude doxologically: *unto God belongs sovereignty* — exclusive, universal, unshared.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:73

> **Certainly they disbelieve, those who say, "Truly God is the third of three." There is no god but one God. And if they desist not from what they say, there shall befall those among them who disbelieve a painful punishment.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Third of Three": The Trinity Verdict**

*Certainly they disbelieve who say: God is the third of three* (*thālithu thalāthah*) — the verdict on trinitarian formulations (cf. 4:171: *say not "three"*; v. 72's incarnation-verdict). *There is no god but one God* (*wāḥid*) — *tawḥīd*'s counter-confession: numerical oneness, undivided essence, unpartnered sovereignty. *If they desist not* (*lam yantahū*) — the conditional holding mercy open (desistance possible, warning purposeful) — *those among them who disbelieve shall meet painful punishment*. Note the precision: *those among them who disbelieve* (*alladhīna kafarū minhum*) — the punishment falls on persisting disbelievers, not indiscriminately on all Christians (the sincere-but-erring, the unreached, the righteous-remnant — 3:113–114 — judged by God's fuller knowledge). The verse verdicts the doctrine definitively while sentencing persons conditionally — severity on propositions, precision on populations. Desist, and mercy; persist, and pain. The terms are stated; the choice is theirs.

**Thālithu Thalāthah: What the Qur'an Addresses**

*Thālithu thalāthah* — "third of three": *which* trinitarianism does the Qur'an address? — the question needs honest handling, for Christian apologists protest misrepresentation. *The Qur'anic data*: 4:171 (*"say not three; desist"*), 5:73 (third-of-three verdict), 5:116 (Jesus questioned: *"did you say: take me and my mother as two gods besides God?"*). *The historical candidates*: Collyridian Marian-excess (Mary venerated quasi-divinely in Arabian peripheries — Epiphanius's *Panarion*), popular tritheism (three-gods confusion among the unlettered), orthodox Trinity misunderstood (Father-Son-Spirit as three gods). *The Qur'an's own framing*: it addresses *actually-held* Arabian beliefs (Mary's quasi-divine veneration — 5:116's specificity suggests real observation) *and* formulates the *tawḥīd*-criterion beneath specifics (any God-numbering violates Oneness — 112:1–4; 4:171's *wāḥid*). *The fair assessment*: orthodox Nicene theology (one essence, three persons — co-equal, co-eternal) *also* falls under the Qur'an's verdict (persons-distinction violates absolute Oneness as Islam defines it), though the Qur'an's *examples* target cruder formulations. *The dialogue-honesty*: Muslims should represent Nicene doctrine accurately (not tritheism-caricature) while verdicting it by *tawḥīd* plainly (not evasion-courtesy). Address realities; apply criteria; leave persons to God.

**"If They Desist Not": The Conditional's Mercy**

*Wa in lam yantahū ʿammā yaqūlūna* — "if they desist not from what they say": the *conditional* — and its mercy-structure needs emphasis, for verdict-verses are invitation-verses. *Desistance defined*: abandoning trinitarian confession (affirming *wāḥid*-Oneness), embracing prophetic finality (Muḥammad ﷺ believed), living righteousness. *Desistance rewarded*: past covered (25:70's conversion — disbelief exchanged for belief, sins for goods), future secured (3:199's scriptured-believers rewarded doubly — own-Book believed *plus* Qur'an). *The conditional's parallels*: Pharaoh warned conditionally (20:44's gentle speech — desistance invited till drowning), Quraysh warned conditionally (conquest-amnesty for desisters), hypocrites warned conditionally (4:146's cure for returners). *The punishment's precision* (*alladhīna kafarū minhum* — those *among them* disbelieving): persisting-deniers sentenced, not communities carpet-condemned — the sincere-erring, unreached, coerced, confused judged by God's encompassing knowledge (22:17; 17:15). *The warner's tone*: verdicts stated (truth untrimmed), invitations extended (mercy unoffered-nowhere), persons un-damned (finality God's). Desist-and-live stands beside persist-and-perish. State both; urge the first; enforce neither (2:256).

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:74

> **Will they not turn in repentance unto God and seek His forgiveness? And God is Forgiving, Merciful.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Invitation After the Verdict: Repentance Offered to Trinitarians**

*A-fa-lā yatūbūna ila'Llāh wa yastaghfirūnah* — "Will they not turn to God and seek His forgiveness?" — the invitation *immediately* following the two severest verdicts (vv. 72–73). The sequence is theology: verdicts diagnose, invitations cure; condemnation without invitation would be cruelty, invitation without verdict would be indifference. *Turn* (*tawbah* — return) *and seek forgiveness* (*istighfār* — covering-request): the same twofold path offered to thieves (v. 39), highwaymen (v. 34), hypocrites (4:146), and all sinners (39:53). No sin's magnitude — not even *shirk*'s (4:48's unforgiven-if-unrepented implies forgiven-if-repented) — exceeds repentance's reach *in this life*. *God is Forgiving* (*Ghafūr*), *Merciful* (*Raḥīm*) — the Names sealing the harshest christological passage in the Qur'an. The pen that wrote *laqad kafara* (certainly disbelieved) writes *Ghafūr Raḥīm* in the same breath. Verdict and mercy, same ink, same page.

**Afalā Yatūbūn: The Question That Woos**

*A-fa-lā yatūbūna* — "will they not turn?": the interrogative-*lā* (*a-fa-lā*: will-they-not?!) — reproach *wooing*, rebuke *beckoning* — and the Qur'an's wooing-questions deserve hearing as love's rhetoric. *The wooing-questions*: *"will they not turn to God and seek forgiveness?"* (this verse — to trinitarians), *"will they not reflect on the Qur'an?"* (4:82; 47:24 — to doubters), *"will they not see?"* (32:27 — to the heedless), *"will they not believe?"* (77:50 — after all evidences), *"will they not remember?"* (10:3 — after cosmic signs). *The rhetoric's psychology*: questions *invite* where commands *compel* — the questioned retains dignity (reason addressed, will respected), the questioner displays longing (God *wanting* return — *"God desires to relent toward you,"* 4:27). *The wooing's object here*: trinitarians verdict-twice — the *least* likely invitees by human reckoning, the *most* pursued by Divine mercy. *The Names' seal* (*Ghafūr, Raḥīm*): forgiveness-and-mercy as wooing's *ground* — return is *rational* because the Returnee forgives. Woo the verdict with questions; seal with Names. *A-fa-lā* — will they not? The question waits eternally; the Names answer perpetually.

**Ghafūr-Raḥīm After Takfīr: Mercy Sealing Severity**

*Wa'Llāhu Ghafūrun Raḥīm* — "God is Forgiving, Merciful": the Names *sealing christological verdicts* — and the juxtaposition's theology needs pondering, for it governs all severity-mercy relations. *The pattern*: severest verdicts (*laqad kafara* — vv. 17, 72, 73), immediately sealed with sweetest invitation (*a-fa-lā yatūbūn* — v. 74) and tenderest Names (*Ghafūr Raḥīm*). *The principle*: revelation never verdicts without inviting — condemnation's *purpose* is return (warnings *aim* at repentance, 39:53–54's *"despair not... turn"*), severity's *limit* is mercy (punishments threatened, pardons offered, 7:156's *"My mercy encompasses all"*). *The parallels*: Pharaoh verdict-yet-invited (20:43–44's gentleness *after* tyranny-proven), Quraysh threatened-yet-amnestied (conquest *after* decades of war), thieves sentenced-yet-received (v. 39's *Ghafūr Raḥīm* after v. 38's amputation). *The warner's model*: verdict-then-invite, always — truth's severity stated (doctrines judged), mercy's sweetness offered (return urged), Names displayed (forgiveness grounded). Severity-without-mercy is cruelty; mercy-without-severity is sentimentality. The verse weds them: *kafara*-verdicts diagnosed, *yatūbūn*-invitation extended, *Ghafūr-Raḥīm* sealing. Same ink, same page, same God.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:75

> **The Messiah, son of Mary, is only a messenger; messengers before him have passed away. And his mother is most truthful. They both used to eat food. Behold how We make the signs clear unto them! Then behold how they are deluded!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Only a Messenger": Christ's True Rank**

*The Messiah, son of Mary, is only* (*illā* — nothing but) *a messenger; messengers before him passed away* (*khalat* — cf. 3:144's Muḥammad-parallel: *"Muḥammad is only a messenger; messengers passed before him"*). The demotion is promotion: messengerhood (*risālah*) is creation's highest rank — Jesus stands with Noah, Abraham, Moses, Muḥammad ﷺ in prophecy's front file (4:163; 33:7's covenant-of-prophets). *His mother is ṣiddīqah* (most truthful — the feminine of *ṣiddīq*, Abū Bakr's title): Mary's rank is supreme *sainthood*, not divinity — truthfulness perfected, worship embodied (3:42–43's chosen-purified, 66:12's Word-believing). *They both used to eat food* (*ya'kulāni'ṭ-ṭaʿām*) — the argument's homely clincher: eaters are needy, the needy are contingent, the contingent are no gods. Divinity neither hungers nor digests. The verse honors both figures truly — prophet and saint — while dethroning both falsely — neither God nor goddess. True rank satisfies; false rank corrupts.

**Ṣiddīqah: Mary's Rank in Islam**

*Wa ummuhū ṣiddīqah* — "his mother is most truthful": *ṣiddīqah* (feminine *ṣiddīq* — supreme truthfulness) — Mary's Islamic rank, and its honors need full inventory, for Islam venerates Mary beyond all women. *The Qur'anic honors*: chosen above all women (*iṣṭafāki ʿalā nisā'i'l-ʿālamīn* — 3:42), purified (*ṭahharaki*), virgin-mother (19:20–21; 21:91: *"We breathed Our Spirit"*), sūrah-named (Sūrah 19 *Maryam* — the only woman sūrah-titled), pain-and-comfort narrated (19:23–26's labor, dates, stream). *The ḥadīth honors*: *"best of women: Mary daughter of ʿImrān"* (al-Bukhārī 3432; Muslim 2431 — with Khadījah, Fāṭimah, Āsiyah completing the four perfect women — al-Bukhārī 3411); Paradise's ladies (Fāṭimah chief — Muslim 2450). *Ṣiddīqiyyah* (supreme truthfulness): belief perfected (66:12: *"she believed her Lord's Words and Books, among the devout"*), word-and-deed aligned, doubt absent. *The boundary*: *ṣiddīqah*, not goddess — veneration's ceiling is creatureliness (worship God's alone — v. 76; 5:116's questioning). Islam gives Mary *everything* but divinity: chosenness, purity, truthfulness, sūrah, Paradise-primacy. Honor her truly: *ṣiddīqah* — most truthful of women, mother of a messenger, servant of God.

**"They Used to Eat Food": Need Refuting Divinity**
