*Kānā ya'kulāni'ṭ-ṭaʿām* — "they both used to eat food": the argument's *homely clincher* — and its philosophical depth needs drawing out, for simplicity here is profundity. *The syllogism*: eaters need (hunger presupposes lack); the needy depend (sustenance from beyond); dependents are contingent (existence derived); the contingent cannot be Necessary (God's *wājib al-wujūd*, necessary existence). Eating → needing → depending → contingent → creature. *The corollaries*: eaters excrete (bodily processes incompatible with transcendence — the commentators state it plainly), eaters hunger (lack is imperfection), eaters die-without (mortality entailed). *The scope*: the argument fells *all* incarnation-claims (Jesus eating), *all* goddess-claims (Mary eating), *all* idol-claims (idols neither eating-nor-benefiting — 21:66–67). *The elegance*: no metaphysics-jargon — *food*, the universal humanizer. Philosophers labor with *wujūd*-proofs; the Qur'an points to dinner. *Unẓur kayfa nubayyinu* (behold how We clarify) — clarity's boast: signs *made clear* (*nubayyin*), expository perfection. *Annā yu'fakūn* (how deluded!) — delusion's marvel: clarity resisted, food ignored, need denied. Behold clarity; marvel at delusion. Eat — and know your Eater-transcending Lord.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:76

> **Say, "Do you worship besides God that which possesses no harm nor benefit for you? And God — He is the Hearing, the Knowing."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Harm nor Benefit: The Impotence Test for Deities**

*Do you worship besides God what possesses for you neither harm* (*ḍarr*) *nor benefit* (*nafʿ*)? — the impotence-test applied to all false gods (cf. 10:18; 13:16; 20:89; 25:3; 34:22). Jesus and Mary — however exalted as messenger and saint — *possess* (*yamlik* — own, control) neither harming nor benefiting *independently*: all power flows from God alone (v. 17's destroying-argument; v. 72's sovereignty-argument). The test is devastatingly practical: prayer answered? protection granted? provision sent? — by the invoked, or by God *through* means? The Qur'an redirects all efficacy Godward: means are veils, God the Actor (*"you threw not when you threw, but God threw,"* 8:17). *God — He is the Hearing* (*Samīʿ* — hearing all invocations) *and Knowing* (*ʿAlīm* — knowing all needs): the true Invoked hears-and-knows; the false invoked neither hear (idols deaf — 21:66–67; dead unhearing — 35:22) nor know. Worship the Hearing-Knowing; abandon the deaf-ignorant. Efficacy decides; God alone efficacious.

**Lā Yamlikūn: Possessionless Intercessors**

*Lā yamlikūna lakum ḍarran wa lā nafʿan* — "possessing for you neither harm nor benefit": *milk* (possession, ownership, control) denied to all besides God — and the denial's scope needs mapping across false-god categories. *Denied to idols*: stones-and-statues owning nothing (25:3: *"they possess neither harm nor benefit, neither death nor life nor resurrection"*). *Denied to saints-and-prophets*: Jesus-and-Mary (this verse), Muḥammad ﷺ himself (*"I possess for you neither harm nor benefit,"* 72:21 — the Prophet's own disclaimer; *"I avail you nothing against God,"* Muslim 206), angels (34:22–23: intercession useless save by leave). *Denied to means*: medicine healing-not-independently, wealth availing-not (26:88), armies prevailing-not (8:17). *Affirmed to God alone*: *"to God belongs all harm-and-benefit"* (10:49's Prophet disclaiming; 7:188: *"I possess not benefit-nor-harm for myself, save what God wills"*). *The intercession-corollary*: intercessors *possess* nothing — intercession *granted* (2:255's *bi-idhnihi*, by His leave), never owned. Worship follows possession: the Possessor worshipped (He hears-knows-acts), the possessionless honored-without-worship (prophets obeyed, saints loved, means used — none invoked). *Mālik* (Possessor) alone; all else possessed.

**Samīʿ-ʿAlīm: The God Who Hears and Knows**

*Wa'Llāhu Huwa's-Samīʿu'l-ʿAlīm* — "God — He is the Hearing, the Knowing": the Names answering impotence — and the pair's worship-logic needs unfolding. *Samīʿ* (All-Hearing): hearing every invocation (2:186: *"I answer the caller's call"*), every whisper (50:16: nearer than jugular), every language simultaneously — the Invoked never deaf (idols deaf — 21:66; the dead unhearing — 35:22). *ʿAlīm* (All-Knowing): knowing every need (2:255's all-encompassing knowledge), every heart's secret (67:13–14), every outcome's wisdom — the Invoked never ignorant. *The pair's argument*: worship requires *hearing* (prayers reaching) and *knowing* (needs comprehended) — the deaf-ignorant (idols, the dead-as-invoked, the absent-as-hearers) fail both; God alone passes both. *The supplication-fruit*: believers invoke confidently (the Hearer hears), trustingly (the Knower knows-best — delays wisdom, denials mercy, 2:216). *Huwa* (He — the pronoun of exclusivity): *He* — none else — hears-knows. Worship the Hearing-Knowing exclusively; honor all else subordinately. Efficacy's address: *Samīʿ-ʿAlīm* — invocation's only competent recipient.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:77

> **Say, "O People of the Book! Exceed not in your religion beyond the truth, and follow not the caprices of a people who went astray aforetime, and who led many astray, and who strayed from the even path."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Ghulūw: Excess Beyond the Truth in Religion**

*Exceed not* (*lā taghlū* — from *ghulūw*, exceeding bounds) *in your religion beyond the truth* (*ghayra'l-ḥaqq*; cf. 4:171) — the Qur'an's standing rebuke of religious excess: deifying Jesus (Christian excess), vilifying him (the opposite excess — 4:156–157), venerating rabbis-as-lords (9:31). *Ghulūw* is truth-overshooting: affirming *more* than revelation warrants (incarnation, Trinity, saint-worship) or *less* (prophet-denial, law-abandonment). The Prophet ﷺ warned his own community identically:

> *"Beware of excess in religion, for those before you were destroyed by excess in religion."* (al-Nasā'ī 3057; Ibn Mājah 3029; Aḥmad — ṣaḥīḥ)

Muslim *ghulūw*-forms: venerating the Prophet ﷺ beyond creatureliness (the very excess this verse rebukes in Christians — *"do not extol me as Christians extolled Jesus; I am God's servant,"* al-Bukhārī 3445), saint-cult excesses, takfīr-excess (declaring Muslims disbelievers for sins), and rigorism (forbidding the permitted — vv. 87–88). *Follow not caprices of a people who went astray before, leading many astray, straying from the even path* (*sawā' al-sabīl*) — triple straying: self, others, path — the forefathers' errors (pagan practices baptized, philosophical speculations canonized) binding descendants. Tradition transmits truth *and* error; revelation sifts both.

**The Prophet's Anti-Ghulūw Charter: "As Christians Extolled"**

*"Lā tuṭrūnī kamā aṭrati'n-Naṣārā ʿĪsā'bna Maryam"* — "extol me not as Christians extolled Jesus son of Mary; I am but God's servant — say: God's servant and messenger" (al-Bukhārī 3445): the Prophet's ﷺ anti-*ghulūw* charter — and its *explicit linkage* to this verse's Christians makes it the section's capstone. *The prohibition*: *iṭrā'* (excessive praise, flattery-beyond-truth) — specifically *christological* excess (raising creature to Creator). *The prescription*: *ʿabd Allāh wa rasūluh* (God's servant *and* messenger) — servanthood *before* messengerhood, creatureliness confessed first. *The applications*: celebrating the Prophet ﷺ (*mawlid*, poetry, praise) bounded by creatureliness (love intensely, worship never); seeking his intercession (*tawassul*-debates bounded — v. 35's lawful modes); venerating his relics-and-grave (respect without worship — *"do not take my grave as an idol,"* Abū Dāwūd 2042; Mālik's *Muwaṭṭa'*). *The irony rebuked*: Muslims committing *exactly* the Christian excess the Qur'an condemns (saint-worship, grave-invocation, prophet-deification fringes) — verse 77's mirror rotated onto ourselves. *The balance*: love without worship (devotion maximal, divinity denied), honor without excess (praise abundant, *iṭrā'* barred). The Prophet ﷺ drew his own ceiling; honor him beneath it. Servant-and-messenger — rank enough for eternity.

**Caprices of the Strayed: Tradition Critiqued**

*Wa lā tattabiʿū ahwā'a qawmin ḍallū min qablu wa aḍallū kathīran wa ḍallū ʿan sawā'i's-sabīl* — "follow not caprices of a people strayed before, leading many astray, straying from the even path": *triple* straying (*ḍallū... aḍallū... ḍallū*) — and tradition's critique needs its methodology, for forefather-following is revelation's perennial foil. *The strayed-before*: pre-Christian paganisms (dying-god myths, divine-son motifs, mother-goddess cults — baptized into christology-periphery), Hellenistic speculations (logos-philosophies, emanation-schemes — canonized into creeds), Israelite rebellions (calf-pattern, prophet-killing — echoed). *Triple straying*: self-strayed (*ḍallū* — erring personally), others-misled (*aḍallū* — erring institutionally — creeds, councils, catechisms transmitting error), path-abandoned (*ḍallū ʿan sawā'* — erring directionally — the even path exited). *The Qur'an's tradition-method*: honor forefathers (Abraham's *millah* — 2:135; 16:123), test traditions (revelation-criterion — v. 48's *muḥaymin*), abandon erring-custom (2:170–171's forefather-refutation: *"though forefathers understood nothing?"*). *Muslim application*: madhhab-loyalty tested (imams commanding *"abandon my word for ḥadīth"* — al-Shāfiʿī's will), cultural customs sifted (un-Islamic traditions abandoned, neutral preserved), scholarly consensus weighed (*ijmāʿ* honored — erring-majorities un-followed). Follow revelation; test tradition; abandon straying — triple-ordered, even-pathed.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:78

> **Cursed are those who disbelieve among the Children of Israel by the tongue of David and Jesus son of Mary. That is because they disobeyed and used to transgress.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Cursed by Two Prophets: David's and Jesus' Curse**

*Cursed* (*luʿina* — Divine curse pronounced) *are disbelievers among Israel by David's tongue and Jesus son of Mary's* — two prophets, one verdict: David cursing Sabbath-violators (transformed to apes — 2:65; 7:166) and Jesus cursing deniers (the Table's threatened punishment — v. 115; or his generation's rejecters). *That is because they disobeyed* (*ʿaṣaw* — rebellion against commands) *and used to transgress* (*yaʿtadūn* — habitual boundary-crossing; present-tense for chronicity). The curse's *agents* matter: not Moses (lawgiver) but David (king-prophet, Psalmist) and Jesus (spirit-prophet, Gospel-bringer) — Israel's *own* beloved prophets pronouncing against Israel's rebels. Internal testimony is unimpeachable: no outsider's bias alleged, no foreigner's ignorance pleaded. The verse also models prophetic severity's legitimacy: David the sweet singer and Jesus the gentle healer *both* cursed persistent rebels — mercy to the repentant never precludes verdicts on the defiant. Love's prophets wielding curse's words: tenderness and severity, same tongues.

**Luʿina: Curse by Prophetic Tongue**

*Luʿina'lladhīna kafarū... ʿalā lisāni Dāwūda wa ʿĪsā'bni Maryam* — "cursed... by David's and Jesus' tongue": *laʿnah* (curse — Divine expulsion from mercy) pronounced *through prophets* — and the mechanism's theology needs care. *Curse defined*: *ṭard* (driving-away) from mercy (*raḥmah*) — the damned's exclusion (2:161–162's curse-abiding), Satan's prototype (15:34–35: *"curse upon thee till Judgment"*). *Prophetic cursing*: Noah cursing his people (71:26–27: *"leave not a single disbeliever"* — after 950 years), Moses cursing Pharaoh's wealth (10:88), David cursing violators (Psalms' imprecations — 109; this verse's Sabbath-curse), Jesus cursing deniers (Gospel-woes — Matthew 23; this verse), Muḥammad ﷺ cursing categories (wine-traders, bribers, usurers — never *named individuals* finally, except revelation-named: Abū Lahab — 111). *The conditions* (prophetic cursing): persistence-proven (mercy exhausted), revelation-guided (prophets curse by Divine leave, not temper), category-or-named-by-God (individuals' final-curse God's alone). *Believers' rule*: curse *categories* (oppressors, disbelief, *ẓulm*), never *named living individuals* finally (their repentance possible — *"curse not,"* the Prophet ﷺ restraining — al-Bukhārī 6046; Muslim 2599: *"the truthful are not cursers"*). David-and-Jesus cursed rebels; believers curse rebellion. Tongues verdicting categories; mercy hoping persons.

**ʿIṣyān and Iʿtidā': Disobedience and Transgression**

*Dhālika bi-mā ʿaṣaw wa kānū yaʿtadūn* — "for disobeying and transgressing": the curse's *causes* — *ʿiṣyān* (disobedience) and *iʿtidā'* (transgression, habitual — *kānū* + present) — and the pair diagnoses damnation's mechanics. *ʿIṣyān* (rebellion against command): Adam's *ʿiṣyān* (20:121 — repented, forgiven), Satan's *ʿiṣyān* (persistent — cursed), Israel's *ʿiṣyān* (calf, Sabbath, prophets — this verse's curse-cause). *Iʿtidā'* (boundary-crossing, aggression): Sabbath-transgressors (*iʿtadaw* — 2:65; 7:163: *"transgressing the Sabbath"*), ḥudūd-crossers (2:229: *"transgressing God's bounds"*), violators habitual (*kānū yaʿtadūn* — transgression *characterized*). *The sequence*: *ʿiṣyān* (command defied) → *iʿtidā'* (boundaries crossed habitually) → *laʿnah* (curse pronounced) — disobedience *hardening* into transgression *earning* exclusion. *The escape*: repentance *before* hardening (Adam's immediacy — 7:23; Israel's calf-repentance accepted — 2:54), desistance *during* (warnings heeded), never despairing (39:53's all-sins). The curse-verse diagnoses causes precisely: disobeyed (commands), transgressed (bounds), habitually. Reverse precisely: obey (commands), honor (bounds), habitually. Causes reversed; curses lifted. *Dhālika* (that is *why*) — causation stated, remedy implied.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:79

> **They would not forbid one another from the iniquity they committed. Evil indeed is what they used to do!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Mutual Non-Forbidding: The Community That Normalized Evil**

*They would not forbid one another* (*lā yatanāhawna* — reciprocal: neither forbidding nor being forbidden) *from the iniquity* (*munkar*) *they committed*. The verse specifies verse 78's curse-cause: the community *normalized* evil by abandoning mutual correction. Each sinner found no warner; each warner found no support; iniquity became atmosphere. *Evil indeed is what they did* (*la-bi'sa mā kānū yafʿalūn*) — the *bi'sa*-verdict (cf. vv. 62–63) falling on *omission*: not-doing (forbidding) verdict like doing (sinning). The Prophet ﷺ illustrated with the ship-parable:

> *"The parable of those observing God's bounds and those violating them is a ship's passengers casting lots: some above, some below. Those below, fetching water, trouble those above — so they decide to drill their own hole. If the above let them, all perish; if they restrain them, all are saved."* (al-Bukhārī 2493, paraphrased)

Mutual forbidding is not mutual meddling; it is mutual rescue. The hole-drillers always plead autonomy; the verse pleads survival.

**Lā Yatanāhawna: Reciprocity of Silence**

*Kānū lā yatanāhawna ʿan munkarin faʿalūh* — "they would not forbid one another any iniquity they did": the *reciprocal* form (*tanāhā* — mutual forbidding) — and its sociology needs unpacking, for silence-pacts structure every corrupt community. *The pact's mechanics*: A sins, B silent (fearing A's retaliation-exposure); B sins, A silent (debt repaid); silence *reciprocated*, iniquity *normalized* — *tanāhī* (mutual forbidding) inverted into *tawāṭu'* (mutual toleration). *The pact's motives*: complicity (sinners cannot rebuke fellow-sinners — glass-houses), cowardice (rebuke costs comfort), cynicism (rebuke deemed futile), tribalism (kin shielded — v. 45's Naḍīr-pattern). *The pact's wages*: *laʿnah* (curse — v. 78), *bi'sa* (evil-verdict — this verse), collective punishment (8:25: *"fear trial afflicting not only wrongdoers"*; the ship sinking *all* — Bukhārī 2493). *The pact's breaker*: reciprocal *courage* — A forbidding B *despite* A's flaws (counsel, not perfection, required), B receiving *despite* A's flaws (truth, not source, weighed) — *tanāṣuḥ* (mutual advising) replacing *tawāṭu'*. The Prophet's ﷺ rule: *"religion is counsel"* (Muslim 55) — *naṣīḥah* universal, reciprocal, perpetual. Forbid mutually; the reciprocity saves mutually.

**The Ship-Parable: Solidarity in Survival**

The Bukhārī-2493 ship-parable — the verse's *ḥadīth*-twin — deserves full exposition, for it founds Islam's *solidarity*-ethics. *The casting*: passengers lot-divided (upper-deck, lower-deck) — society stratified (scholars-masses, rulers-ruled, righteous-sinners) *legitimately*. *The trouble*: lower fetching water disturbs upper — interdependence inescapable (sinners' sins *affect* righteous — corruption's externalities). *The hole*: lower deciding self-reliance (drilling for direct water) — autonomy's plea (*"our* deck, *our* choice") masking collective suicide. *The choice*: upper intervening (restraint — *nahy* — paternalism saving) or permitting (tolerance — *tasāmuḥ* — permissiveness drowning). *The verdict*: restrain → *all* saved (forbidders-and-forbidden alike); permit → *all* perished (tolerant-and-drillers together). *The applications*: drug-liberalization (holes drilled), financial fraud (hulls breached), public indecency (decks flooded), extremism (ships hijacked) — every *"private choice"* with *public hull*. *Lā yatanāhawna* (non-forbidding) is hole-permitting; *tanāhī* is hull-preserving. Restrain drillers; save ships. Autonomy ends at hulls; survival demands mutuality.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:80

> **Thou seest many of them taking disbelievers as allies. Evil indeed is what they send forth for themselves, that God is wrathful with them, and they shall abide in punishment.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Allies of Disbelievers: The Verse's Treason Charge**

*Thou seest many of them taking disbelievers* (*alladhīna kafarū*) *as allies* (*yatawallawnahum*) — hypocrites (or hostile Jews) allying pagans against Muslims (Ṭ, Z; cf. vv. 51–52). *Evil indeed is what they send forth* (*qaddamū* — forwarded, invested) *for themselves* — deeds as advance-consignments to the Hereafter (cf. 2:110; 73:20: *send forth good, find it with God*). *That God is wrathful* (*sakhiṭa* — wrath, indignation) *with them, abiding in punishment* (*khālidūn*). The verse's economics: alliances are *investments* — hedgers (v. 52) investing in tribes, believers investing in God (v. 55–56). Portfolios mature hereafter: tribal stock crashes (confederates useless — 26:93; 43:67: *friends that Day are enemies*), Divine stock pays (56's *ghālibūn*-victors). *Sakhaṭ* (wrath) here answers alliance-betrayal specifically: loyalty sold to God's enemies purchases God's enmity. Invest loyalties where eternity honors them.

**Qaddamū: Deeds as Advance Consignments**

*Mā qaddamat lahum anfusuhum* — "what their souls sent forth for them": *taqdīm* (sending-ahead) — deeds as *advance consignments* — and the Qur'an's investment-imagery needs its portfolio-theory. *The mechanism*: every deed *forwarded* (2:110: *"send forth good for yourselves, find it with God"*; 73:20; 99:7–8's atom-weights), the Hereafter receiving consignments daily. *Good consignments*: prayers (2:110), charity (2:261's seven-hundredfold), patience (39:10's unreckoned wages), alliance-with-God (vv. 55–56's victor-stock). *Evil consignments*: this verse's hostile-alliances (*bi'sa mā qaddamū* — evil forwarding), disbelief (3:117's wind-blasted harvest), oppression (agents' wages reversed — 14:42–43). *The portfolio-review*: 59:18 (*"let every soul consider what it sent forth for tomorrow"* — daily auditing commanded), 36:12 (*"We record what they sent forth and their traces"* — consignments *plus* legacies). *The verse's audit*: hostile-alliance stock — *bi'sa* (evil-rated), *sakhaṭ*-dividends (wrath-paying), *khulūd*-maturity (perpetual loss). Hedgers diversified into wrath; believers concentrated in victory. Audit nightly; consign daily; mature eternally.

**Sakhaṭ Allāh: Divine Wrath's Occasions**

*An sakhiṭa'Llāhu ʿalayhim* — "God is wrathful with them": *sakhaṭ* (wrath, indignation, anger-at-betrayal) — and its Qur'anic occasions need cataloguing, for wrath is *earned*, never capricious. *Wrath-occasions*: alliance-with-enemies (this verse), hypocrisy (9:68's promised Fire; 48:6's wrath-and-curse on hypocrites-and-polytheists), disbelief-after-belief (16:106: *"wrath from God"* on coerced-excepted apostates), prophet-killing (2:61's wrath-upon-wrath), pleasure-of-enemies sought (47:28: *"following what angers God, hating His pleasure"*). *Wrath's nature*: not temper (God transcends passion — *laysa ka-mithlihi*, 42:11) but *judicial* indignation — betrayal's fitting response, holiness's necessary recoil. *Wrath's signs*: abandonment (enemies empowered — v. 80's allies useless), hardening (hearts sealed — 4:155), disgrace (v. 41's worldly *khizy*). *Wrath's escape*: desistance (wrath *earned* is wrath *escapable* — 39:53's despair-ban), pleasing-God (47:28 inverted: following His pleasure, hating His anger), repentance (wrath lifted — 20:86's calf-wrath then 7:153's pardon). *Wa fi'l-ʿadhābi hum khālidūn* (abiding in torment): wrath's *maturity* — unrepented betrayal's perpetual dividend. Earn never; escape always-possible-till-death. Consign loyalties Godward.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:81

> **And had they believed in God and the Prophet, and what was sent down unto him, they would not have taken them as allies. But many of them are iniquitous.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Belief Precludes Betrayal: The Incompatibility Thesis**

*Had they believed in God and the Prophet* (*nabī*) *and what was sent to him, they would not have taken them as allies* — belief and hostile-alliance are *incompatible*: genuine faith in God-and-Prophet *precludes* confederating with their enemies (cf. 58:22: *believers do not befriend God's-and-Messenger's opposers, even kin*). The conditional (*law kānū yu'minūn*) exposes profession's hollowness: alliance-behavior *disproves* belief-claims — *walāyah*-choices X-ray *īmān*-realities. *But many of them are fāsiqūn* (iniquitous — persistently departing; cf. vv. 47, 49, 59) — the majority-verdict closing the section (vv. 78–81): curse earned (78), correction abandoned (79), enemies allied (80), belief voided (81). Four verses, one autopsy: disobedience → silence → betrayal → unbelief. The pathology progresses; the verse arrests it at diagnosis. Believe genuinely; ally accordingly. Incompatibility absolute; iniquity optional.

**Law Kānū Yu'minūn: Behavior Proving Belief**

*Wa law kānū yu'minūna bi'Llāhi wa'n-nabiyyi... ma'ttakhadhūhum awliyā'* — "had they believed... they would not ally": the conditional's *evidentiary* logic — behavior *proving* (or disproving) belief — and the principle needs its jurisprudence, for claims-versus-conduct adjudicates everywhere. *The principle*: genuine belief *necessarily* produces compliant conduct (not perfectly — sins occur — but *directionally*: believers sin-and-return, hypocrites persist-and-ally). *Qur'anic applications*: 58:22 (believers befriend-not opposers — loyalty-proving), 49:15 (believers doubt-not, strive — striving-proving), 9:44–45 (true believers excuse-not — participation-proving), 4:65 (no-belief till arbitration-accepted — submission-proving). *The converse-caution*: conduct-failures don't *automatically* void belief (sinners remain believers — 49:10's brotherhood; 4:48's forgivable) — the verse targets *alliance-patterns* (persistent confederation), not *sin-episodes*. *The diagnostic use*: self-audit (do my alliances prove my beliefs?), community-assessment (patterns weighed, episodes pardoned), never individual-*takfīr* (final verdicts God's — 22:17). Behavior evidences; patterns prove; episodes except. Had they believed, they'd ally otherwise. Believe — then ally like it.

**Fāsiqūn Again: The Section's Closing Census**

*Wa lākinna kathīran minhum fāsiqūn* — "but many of them are iniquitous": *fisq* (persistent departure, cf. vv. 47, 49, 59) — the section's *closing census* (vv. 78–81) — and the census-pattern across the sūrah needs noting. *The censuses*: v. 32 (*many prodigal* — *musrifūn* — after messengers), v. 49 (*many iniquitous* — after judgment-offer), v. 59 (*most iniquitous* — resenting belief), v. 66 (*many evil-doing* — after prosperity-offer), v. 81 (*many iniquitous* — after belief-test). *The pattern*: each invitation extended (messengers, judgment, dialogue, prosperity, alliance-test), each majority declining — *kathīr/kathīran* (many) verdicting, remnants excepted (v. 66's *muqtaṣidah*; 3:113–114's upright). *The theology*: majorities decline (12:103: *"most believe not"*; 17:89; 34:13's grateful-few) — truth's unpopularity *normal*, not anomalous. *The pastoral*: warners mustn't majority-calibrate (numbers never validate — 6:116: *"most on earth mislead"*), believers mustn't majority-despair (remnants suffice — Noah's few, Ṭālūt's drinkers-abstaining-few, Badr's 313). *Fisq* as majority-state: departure *normalized*, persistence *expected*. Census the section; join the remnant. Many depart; few arrive. Arrive.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:82

> **Thou wilt surely find the most hostile of people towards those who believe to be the Jews and the polytheists. And thou wilt surely find the nearest of them in affection towards those who believe to be those who say, "We are Christians." That is because among them are priests and monks, and because they are not arrogant.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Hostility and Affection: The Verse's Historical Observation**

*Thou wilt surely find most hostile* (*ashadda ʿadāwah*) *toward believers: the Jews and polytheists* (*alladhīna ashrakū*) — *and nearest in affection* (*aqraba mawaddah*): *those saying "we are Christians"* (*naṣārā*). The verse *observes* (empirical *latajidanna* — "thou wilt surely find") the Prophet's ﷺ historical experience: Madinan Jews' sustained hostility (plots, mockery, confederacies — vv. 41–81's dossier) and Makkan pagans' persecution, contrasted with Abyssinian Christians' refuge (the Najāshī sheltering Muslims, weeping at Sūrah Maryam — vv. 82–83's occasion) and Najrān's dialogue. *Because among them are priests* (*qissīsīn*) *and monks* (*ruhbān*), *and they are not arrogant* (*lā yastakbirūn*) — learning-and-humility producing receptivity. The verse is descriptive-historical, not prescriptive-eternal: it characterizes encountered populations, not essential natures — proven by righteous Jews praised (3:113–114; 7:159) and hostile Christians condemned (9:29–35's context; v. 14's covenant-breakers) elsewhere. History's snapshot; not eternity's stereotype.

**Latajidanna: Finding as Empirical Method**

*La-tajidanna ashadda... wa la-tajidanna aqraba...* — "thou wilt surely find most-hostile... nearest...": *wijdān* (finding, empirical discovery) — revelation *observing* history — and the method needs appreciating, for the Qur'an *reasons* from experience. *The findings*: hostility-ranked (Jews-and-polytheists most-hostile *in the Prophet's ﷺ experience* — Madinan plots, Makkan wars), affection-ranked (Christians nearest *in the Prophet's ﷺ experience* — Abyssinian refuge, Najrān dialogue, Egyptian *Muqawqis*-gifts). *The causes*: analyzed (priests-and-monks — learned devotion; non-arrogance — humility — producing Christian receptivity; rabbinic-polemical traditions and pagan-tribalism producing others' hostility). *The method's honesty*: revelation *could* stereotype (essential natures fixed); instead it *reports* (historical encounters described) — leaving later histories open (Christian Crusades-hostility, Jewish Andalusian-conviviality — both post-verse, both accommodated by descriptivism). *The abuse-guard*: prescriptivizing the descriptive (eternalizing hostility/affection) violates the method — *latajidanna* (thou *findest*) binds to *finding*, not *fixing*. Find honestly; report accurately; update perpetually. Empirical revelation; open history.

**Qissīsīn and Ruhbān: Priests, Monks, Humility**

*Dhālika bi-anna minhum qissīsīna wa ruhbānan wa annahum lā yastakbirūn* — "for among them are priests and monks, and they are not arrogant": the affection's *causes* — learning, devotion, humility — and each cause needs its appreciation. *Qissīsīn* (priests, from *q-s-s*: tracing, narrating — scholars tracing scripture): learned Christianity (scripture-studied, doctrine-refined) producing *recognition-capacity* (scholars recognizing truth — v. 83's tears; Ibn Salām-pattern). *Ruhbān* (monks, from *rahbah*: awe, fear — God-fearing devotees): ascetic Christianity (world-renounced, worship-devoted) producing *receptivity-capacity* (hearts softened — v. 83's *riqqah*). *Lā yastakbirūn* (non-arrogant): humility (status un-clung, correction wel­comed) producing *submission-capacity* (truth embraced without pride-barrier — contrast Pharaoh's *istikbār* — 10:75; Quraysh's — 25:21). *The triad's power*: learning-without-arrogance (scholars humble), devotion-without-arrogance (monks gentle), humility-throughout — receptivity's complete recipe. *The Muslim mirror*: scholars (*qissīs*-parallels) humble? devotees (*rāhib*-parallels) gentle? communities arrogance-free? Receptivity's recipe transfers. Learn, devote, humble — affection follows.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:83

> **And when they hear what has been sent down unto the Messenger, thou seest their eyes overflow with tears because of the truth they recognize. They say, "Our Lord, we believe, so inscribe us among the witnesses."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Tears of Recognition: The Weeping Christians**

*When they hear what was sent to the Messenger, thou seest eyes overflowing* (*tafīḍu* — flooding, brimming) *with tears* (*damʿ*) *from recognizing truth* (*mimmā ʿarafū mina'l-ḥaqq*) — the Najāshī's delegation (or Abyssinian converts) weeping at Qur'an-recitation (Ṭ, W; cf. 28:52–53; 17:107–109's prostrating-weepers). *Our Lord, we believe — inscribe us among witnesses* (*shāhidīn* — cf. 3:53's disciples: *"inscribe us with witnesses"*). Tears-as-recognition: truth *known* (scripture-studied — priests' learning, v. 82) *recognized* (Qur'an matching Injīl-strata — *muḥaymin*-confirmation, v. 48) *felt* (hearts softened — monks' devotion, v. 82) *confessed* (belief declared, witness requested). The verse canonizes weeping-as-worship: tears shed for truth rank among devotion's purest expressions (cf. 19:58; 53:60–61's rebuked laughter). Eyes flooding, hearts confessing, names inscribed. Recognition's liturgy; tears' theology.

**Tafīḍu mina'd-Damʿ: Tears in Revelation's Register**

*Tafīḍu aʿyunuhum mina'd-damʿi mimmā ʿarafū mina'l-ḥaqq* — "eyes flooding with tears from truth recognized": *damʿ* (tears) in revelation's *register* — and the tear-theology needs its full liturgy. *Praiseworthy tears*: recognition-tears (this verse — Christians weeping), awe-tears (17:107–109: *"falling chin-ward, weeping, humility increased"*), mercy-tears (the Prophet's ﷺ weeping for the dead — Muslim 2315; for the *ummah* — Muslim 202), fear-tears (*"two eyes Fire touches not: weeping from God's fear,"* al-Tirmidhī 1639), love-tears (Abū Bakr's *Ghar*-weeping — joy-and-fear mingled). *Blameworthy non-tears*: hard hearts tearless (2:74's stone-hearts; 57:16's hearts hardened through time), mockery-laughter (53:59–61: *"do you marvel, laughing, weeping not?"*). *Tears' physiology-spiritual*: eyes *flooding* (*fayḍ* — overflowing abundance) — emotion exceeding vessels, truth overwhelming containers. *Mimmā ʿarafū* (from *recognizing* — *maʿrifah*-rooted): tears of *knowledge*, not sentimentality — priests' learning (v. 82) flowering into weeping. Know truly; feel deeply; weep freely. Tearless eyes suspect; flooded eyes inscribed.

**"Inscribe Us Among Witnesses": The Confession's Crown**

*Rabbanā āmannā fa'ktubnā maʿa'sh-shāhidīn* — "our Lord, we believe — inscribe us with witnesses": the confession's *crown* — belief declared, witness *requested* — and the request's parallels need hearing. *The disciples' confession* (3:53): *"our Lord, we believed what Thou sent, following the Messenger — inscribe us with witnesses"* — *identical* request (Christian believers' liturgy, Qur'anically quoted twice). *The witness-rank* (*shahādah*): testifying truth (2:143's witness-nation; 4:41's Prophet-witnessing; 22:78), martyrdom (3:140's martyrs — *shuhadā'*-witnesses through blood), eschatological testifying (limbs witnessing — 36:65; prophets witnessing — 4:41). *Faktubnā* (inscribe us — *kitābah*: writing, registering): enrollment *requested* (Divine registry — 83:18–21's *ʿilliyyīn*-record), humility's form (asking-inscription, not claiming-status). *The verse's arc* (82–83): priests-and-monks (learning-devotion) → non-arrogance (humility) → hearing (reception) → recognizing (knowledge) → weeping (feeling) → confessing (belief) → requesting (witness) — receptivity's *complete* liturgy, sevenfold. Hear, recognize, weep, confess, request. Inscription follows; witnesses enrolled.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:84

> **"And why should we not believe in God and the truth that has come unto us, and hope that our Lord will admit us with the righteous people?"**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Why Should We Not Believe?": The Convert's Logic**

*Why should we not believe* (*mā lanā lā nu'minu*) *in God and the truth come to us, hoping our Lord admits us with the righteous* (*ṣāliḥīn*)? — the converts' rhetorical question: belief's *rationality* proclaimed (what hinders? nothing), hope's *audacity* voiced (admission with righteous *hoped* — *naṭmaʿu*, craving). The logic inverts habitual convert-narratives: not "despite costs, we believe" but "given truth, how could we not?" Costs unmentioned (persecution, exile, loss — Abyssinians knew them); truth's compulsion total. *With the righteous* (*maʿa'l-qawmi'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn*): company hoped — salvation *social* (admission *with*, community joined), righteousness *located* (the ṣāliḥīn identifiable — prophets, martyrs, saints — 4:69). The verse teaches converts' apologetic: don't justify belief as leap — proclaim it as logic. Truth come, recognition certain, hope blazing. Why *not* believe? No reason remains.

**Mā Lanā Lā Nu'min: Faith's Rational Audacity**

*Wa mā lanā lā nu'minu bi'Llāhi wa mā jā'anā mina'l-ḥaqq* — "what is with us, that we believe not?": the question's *rational audacity* — faith as *reason's* demand — and the audacity needs its philosophy. *The question's form*: *mā lanā* (what ails us? what excuse?) — excuse-demanding (no excuse found), rationality-asserting (belief *reasonable*). *The question's content*: God believed (tawḥīd evident), truth-come believed (*ḥaqq jā'* — arrival acknowledged) — premises granted, conclusion compelled. *Qur'anic parallels*: 36:22 (*"why should I not worship Him who originated me?"* — the believing man's logic), 67:8–9 (warners-come acknowledged, denial inexcusable), 76:3 (path shown — grateful-or-ingrate). *The philosophy*: faith is *rational* (evidences compelling — *ḥaqq* recognized, v. 83), disbelief *irrational* (excuses absent — *mā lanā* unanswered). Converts needn't apologize for believing (leaps, gambles, wishes); deniers must answer for disbelieving (evidences spurned, truth recognized-yet-refused). *Naṭmaʿu* (we hope-crave — *ṭamaʿ*: eager hoping): hope's *appetite* — admission *craved* (not merely wished), righteousness-company *hungered*. Believe rationally; hope hungrily. *Mā lanā* — no excuse; *naṭmaʿ* — every hope.

**Maʿa'ṣ-Ṣāliḥīn: Company of the Righteous**

*An yudkhilanā Rabbanā maʿa'l-qawmi'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* — "our Lord admitting us *with* the righteous people": salvation's *sociality* — admission *with* — and the company's theology needs its roster. *The ṣāliḥīn* (righteous): prophets-and-followers (12:101's Joseph: *"join me with righteous"*; 27:19's Solomon; 29:9, 27), martyrs-and-saints (4:69's company: prophets, truthful, martyrs, righteous — *ḥasuna ulā'ika rafīqan*, excellent companions), the universally-pious (3:114's night-praying remnant). *Maʿa* (with-ness): salvation *communal* (admission *into company*, not isolation — Gardens *shared*, thrones *facing* — 15:47; 37:44), righteousness *contagious* (company elevating — *"man is upon his friend's religion,"* Abū Dāwūd 4833; al-Tirmidhī 2378), hope *located* (the righteous *identifiable* — join them *now*, admitted *with them then*). * Conversions' sociality*: Abyssinians converting *together* (delegation-belief), Anṣār converting *tribally*, families converting *wholly* (Noah's household-command — 11:40; Lot's — 15:59–60) — belief *shared*, admission *shared*. Hope company; join company; admitted into company. *Maʿa'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn* — with-ness hoped, with-ness lived, with-ness granted.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:85

> **So God rewarded them for what they said with Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding therein. And that is the reward of the virtuous.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Rewarded "For What They Said": Words That Earn Gardens**

*God rewarded them for what they said* (*bi-mā qālū*) — *for saying*: Gardens for *confession* (vv. 83–84's belief declared, witness requested, hope voiced). Words earning eternity: the *shahādah*'s worldly-utterance (Paradise-earning — *"whoever's last words are lā ilāha illa'Llāh enters Gardens,"* Abū Dāwūd 3116), the converts' confession (this verse), the martyrs' testimony (blood-witnessing). *Gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding therein* (*khālidīn*) — perpetuity for sincerity-moment: confession's instant purchasing eternity. *That is the virtuous'* (*muḥsinīn*) *reward* — confession-classed as *iḥsān* (virtuous excellence): belief *beautifully* declared (tears, humility, hope — vv. 82–84's liturgy) earning excellence's wage. The verse dignifies saying: speech is deed (*qawl* weighed — 50:18's recorded word), confession is currency (Gardens purchased), witness-requests are investments (inscription granted). Say truly; earn eternally. Words matter infinitely.

**Bi-Mā Qālū: Speech as Salvation-Currency**

*Fa-athābahumu'Llāhu bi-mā qālū* — "God rewarded them *for what they said*": *qawl* (speech) as *salvation-currency* — and the doctrine needs its treasury, for words purchase destinies. *Saving speech*: the *shahādah* (keys-of-Paradise — al-Bukhārī 5827; Muslim 26–27), converts' confessions (this verse — Gardens *for saying*), truth-testimonies (martyrs' *shahādah* — blood-sealed speech), dhikr-utterances (*Subḥāna'Llāh* filling-scales — Muslim 223; *lā ilāha illa'Llāh* outweighing — Ibn Mājah 3797). *Damning speech*: disbelief-declarations (Pharaoh's *"I am your lord most high,"* 79:24), mockery (v. 57–58's *istihzā'*), false oaths (v. 53's *jahd*), backbiting (*ḥasanāt*-devouring — Muslim 2581's bankrupt). *The recording*: *"no word uttered but a watcher ready"* (50:18) — speech *audited* (every utterance registered), weighed (50:18's *raqīb-ʿatīd*), recompensed (this verse's Gardens-for-saying). *The excellence* (*muḥsinīn*-reward): confession *beautifully* performed (tears — v. 83, logic — v. 84, humility — v. 82) — *iḥsān* in speech (words excellently uttered) earning excellence's wage. Speak savingly; the currency spends eternally. *Bi-mā qālū* — for *saying*, Gardens.

**Jazā' al-Muḥsinīn: Excellence's Wage**

*Wa dhālika jazā'u'l-muḥsinīn* — "that is the virtuous' reward": *iḥsān* (virtuous excellence — worshipping *as though seeing*, Muslim 8's *iḥsān*-definition) — and its wage-structure needs exhibiting. *The wage*: Gardens-rivers-abiding (this verse) — excellence earning perpetuity. *The wages elsewhere*: *"is excellence's reward but excellence?"* (55:60 — *hal jazā'u'l-iḥsāni illa'l-iḥsān* — the Qur'an's most beautiful wage-formula), increased (*ziyādah* — 10:26: excellence-plus-more — the Beatific Vision, per exegesis), Divine love (2:195; 3:134, 148: *God loves muḥsinīn*), Divine company (29:69: *"God is with muḥsinīn"*). *The excellence here*: converts' confession-excellence — truth recognized (knowledge), tears shed (feeling), belief declared (courage), witness requested (humility), hope voiced (trust) — *iḥsān* fivefold. *The lesson*: excellence isn't perfectionism (flawless performance) but beautiful devotion (wholehearted offering) — converts' first-confession out-earning veterans' routine. Worship as-though-seeing; confess as-though-inscribed; hope as-though-admitted. Excellence waged excellently; Gardens for beauty.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:86

> **And those who disbelieve and deny Our signs, they are the inhabitants of Hellfire.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Denial's Destination: The Verse's Stark Verdict**

*Those who disbelieve* (*kafarū*) *and deny Our signs* (*kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā*) — disbelief-plus-denial (heart rejecting, tongue refuting) — *they are Hellfire's inhabitants* (*aṣḥāb al-jaḥīm*). The verse's starkness is structural: verse 85's Gardens-for-confession demands verse 86's Fire-for-denial — the converts' reward-context (vv. 82–85) requiring the deniers' verdict-context. *Aṣḥāb* (companions, inhabitants — from *ṣuḥbah*, companionship): Fire *companioned* (dwelling-with, as spouses *aṣḥāb*, friends *aṣḥāb*) — damnation as *association* (the damned *keeping company* with flames). The verse verdicts *categories* (disbelieving-deniers), not *individuals* (persons judged by God — 22:17) — propositions sentenced, persons deferred. Starkness purposeful: choices clarified (confess-or-deny), destinations displayed (Gardens-or-Fire), decisions urged. Deny signs; inhabit Fire. Confess truth; inhabit Gardens. Stark, just, clear.

**Kafarū wa Kadhdhabū: Disbelief Plus Denial**

*Wa'lladhīna kafarū wa kadhdhabū bi-āyātinā* — "disbelieving and denying Our signs": the *doubled* verdict (*kufr* + *takdhīb*) — and the doubling's significance needs parsing. *Kufr* (disbelief, covering): heart's rejection — truth covered (ingratitude, concealment, refusal). *Takdhīb* (denial, calling-lie): tongue's refutation — truth *contradicted* (messengers called liars — 23:33; signs called sorcery — 74:24; resurrection called fable — 83:13). *The doubling*: heart *and* tongue (inward rejection *plus* outward refutation) — damnation's full formula (contrast heart-rejecting-tongue-silent: hypocrisy's variant; tongue-confessing-heart-doubting: weak faith's struggle). *Qur'anic frequency*: the pair recurs as damnation's standard formula (2:39; 3:12; 5:10, 86; 8:52, 54; 22:57; 57:19; 64:10; 90:19) — verdict-liturgy's refrain. *The signs* (*āyātinā*): revelation-verses (Qur'an denied), cosmic-signs (creation's testimony refused), miraculous-signs (prophets' proofs rejected) — *Our* signs (Divine possession — denying *God's*). Disbelieve inwardly, deny outwardly — Fire companioned. Believe inwardly, confess outwardly (v. 85) — Gardens entered. Doubled-destinies for doubled-responses.

**Aṣḥāb al-Jaḥīm: Companionship with Fire**

*Ulā'ika aṣḥābu'l-jaḥīm* — "those are Hellfire's companions": *ṣuḥbah* (companionship) with *jaḥīm* (blazing Fire) — damnation as *association* — and the companionship-imagery needs its horror. *Aṣḥāb*-usages: Garden-companions (2:82; 7:42 — bliss associated), Fire-companions (2:39; 5:10, 86 — torment associated), Cave-companions (18:9 — refuge shared), Elephant-companions (105:1 — destruction shared), Heights-companions (7:46–48 — waiting stationed). *Companionship's intimacy*: *ṣāḥib* is intimate associate (spouse — 80:36; friend — 18:34; fellow-traveler) — Fire *intimately* associated (flames embracing, 104:6–7's *encompassing*; 90:20's *closed-over*). *Jaḥīm* (from *ja-ḥ-m*: fierce blazing): Fire's fiercest register ( pavement-cracking heat — 26:91's *burrizat*; 81:12's *suʿʿirat*, kindled-fierce). *The contrast* (85–86): *muḥsinīn*-rewarded (Gardens companioned — bliss associated) versus deniers-verdicts (Fire companioned — torment associated) — companionships *chosen* (confession-or-denial), eternally kept. Choose companions now (righteous — v. 84's *maʿa'ṣ-ṣāliḥīn*); companioned then (Gardens-or-Fire). Association's wages; companionship's eternities.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:87

> **O you who believe! Forbid not the good things that God has made lawful for you, and transgress not. Truly God loves not transgressors.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Forbidding the Lawful: Asceticism Rebuked**

*Forbid not* (*lā tuḥarrimū*) *the good things* (*ṭayyibāt*) *God made lawful* (*aḥalla*) — the verse rebukes self-imposed asceticism: Companions (ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn's circle) vowing celibacy, meat-abandonment, sleep-renunciation, continuous fasting (Ṭ, W; cf. 66:1's honey-prohibition). The Prophet ﷺ summoned and corrected: *"I fast and break, pray and sleep, marry women — whoever turns from my way is not of me"* (al-Bukhārī 5063; Muslim 1401, paraphrased). *Transgress not* (*lā taʿtadū* — cf. v. 78's *iʿtidā'*) — forbidding-lawful *is* transgression (boundary-crossing Godward — legislating where God legislated). *God loves not transgressors* (*muʿtadīn*) — Divine love *withheld* (contrast v. 42's *loves the just*; v. 54's mutual love). The verse founds Islam's world-affirmation: creation's goods *enjoyed* (worship's complement), lawful pleasures *embraced* (gratitude's expression), asceticism *bounded* (prophetic moderation). Forbid never what God permitted; transgress never where God bounded.

**Ṭayyibāt: The Good Things' Theology**

*Mā aḥalla'Llāhu lakum mina'ṭ-ṭayyibāt* — "the good things God made lawful": *ṭayyibāt* (good, pure, wholesome things) — and their theology grounds Islam's *world-affirmation*. *Ṭayyibāt*-catalogue: lawful foods (2:168, 172: *"eat ṭayyibāt"*; 5:4–5's game-and-scriptured-food), marriage (4:3's lawful spouses), adornment (7:32: *"who forbade God's adornment?"*), wealth (lawful earnings — 2:267's *ṭayyibāt*-spending), rest (sleep as mercy — 30:23; 78:9). *Ṭayyibāt* versus *khabā'ith* (foul things — 7:157: the Prophet ﷺ permitting-ṭayyibāt, forbidding-khabā'ith): creation *sorted* (pure enjoyed, foul avoided), not creation *shunned* (all enjoyed) nor *demonized* (all avoided). *The forbidding-prohibition*: *lā tuḥarrimū* — humans legislating-forbiddance *usurp* Divine prerogative (16:116: *"say not: this lawful, this forbidden — forging lies"*; 10:59; 6:138–140's pagan forbiddances condemned). *The enjoyment-command*: *kulū* (eat! — v. 88; 2:168; 7:31: *"eat, drink, exceed not"*), *ishrabū*, *tamttaʿū* (enjoy — 3:14's adornments-acknowledged). Enjoy gratefully; forbid never; exceed never. *Ṭayyibāt* gifted; gratitude returning; asceticism rebuked.

**Lā Taʿtadū: Transgression Godward**

*Wa lā taʿtadū* — "transgress not": *iʿtidā'* (boundary-crossing) *Godward* — legislating where God legislated — and the Godward-transgression needs its warning. *Transgression's directions*: manward (oppressing creatures — 2:190's fighting-limits; 4:153's Sabbath; v. 79's iniquity), Godward (legislating beside God — this verse's forbidding-lawful; permitting-forbidden — 9:31's rabbis-as-lords; worship-innovating — *bidʿah*'s legislation). *This verse's iʿtidā'*: ascetic legislation (celibacy-vows, meat-bans, sleep-oaths) — piety *overshooting* (v. 77's *ghulūw*-parallel), devotion *legislating* (vows binding where God loosed). *Inna'Llāha lā yuḥibbu'l-muʿtadīn* (God loves not transgressors): love *withheld* — the devotee's terror (beloved-ness forfeited over piety-excess). *The correction* (Prophetic way): fast-and-break, pray-and-sleep, marry (Bukhārī 5063) — moderation *modeled*, extremism *disowned* (*"not of me"* — membership revoked). Piety bounded; legislation God's; moderation prophetic. Transgress neither manward (oppression) nor Godward (legislation). Love's condition: bounds honored.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:88

> **And eat of what God has provided for you, lawful and good. And reverence God, in Whom you believe.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Eat Lawful and Good: The Enjoyment Command**

*Eat* (*kulū*) *of what God provided* (*razaqakum*) — *lawful* (*ḥalāl*) *and good* (*ṭayyib*) — the enjoyment *command* (imperative *kulū*): eating-lawful as *obedience* (revelation commanding pleasure), provision as *Divine* (*razaqa* — God the Provider — 51:58's *Razzāq*). *Ḥalāl-and-ṭayyib* (lawful-and-good): the dual criterion (lawfulness *necessary* — pork, carrion, wine excluded; goodness *complementary* — wholesome, clean, beneficial preferred). *Reverence God, in Whom you believe* (*wattaqū'Llāha'lladhī antum bihi mu'minūn*) — *taqwā* as enjoyment's *guard* (pleasure bounded — v. 87's *lā taʿtadū*), belief as reverence's *ground* (*in Whom you believe* — faith motivating fear). The verse completes the asceticism-correction (vv. 87–88): forbid-never (87), enjoy-always-lawfully (88), revere-perpetually (88b). Three imperatives: *lā tuḥarrimū* (forbid-not), *kulū* (eat!), *ittaqū* (revere!). Asceticism rebuked; enjoyment commanded; reverence guarding.

**Razaqakum: Provision's Theology**

*Mimmā razaqakumu'Llāh* — "of what God provided you": *rizq* (provision) — and its theology transforms eating into *worship*. *The Provider*: God *ar-Razzāq* (51:58: the Great Provider; 11:6: every creature's provision *upon God*; 29:60), providing believers-and-deniers (17:20: *"all aided — these and those"*), humans-and-beasts (22:58; 24:41's birds). *The provision's scope*: food (this verse), wealth (2:212), spouses-children (16:72), knowledge (20:114's increase-requested), all-good (16:53: *"whatever blessing, from God"*). *The gratitude-duty*: provision *recognized* (16:114: *"be grateful for God's blessings"*), *shared* (zakāh-charity — provision circulated), *lawfully-sought* (means honest — *suḥt* avoided, vv. 42, 62–63). *The anxiety-cure*: provision *guaranteed* (11:6; *"no soul dies till completing provision,"* Ibn Mājah 2144, ṣaḥīḥ) — anxiety's antidote (trust *tawakkul* — 65:3's sufficiency), greed's rebuke (hoarding condemned — 9:34; 104:2–3). Eat-as-provided (gratitude), seek-as-trusting (effort-with-reliance), share-as-circulating (blessing spread). *Razaqakum* — provided *you* (personally, particularly). Provisioned gratefully; anxiety cured; greed rebuked.

**Ḥalāl-Ṭayyib: The Dual Criterion**

*Ḥalālan ṭayyiban* — "lawful and good": the *dual* criterion — lawfulness *and* wholesomeness — and the duality's dietary (and beyond) applications need drawing. *Ḥalāl* (lawful): revelation-permitted (slaughtered properly — v. 3; untampered — non-carrion, non-blood, non-pork, non-dedicated; earned-lawfully — *suḥt*-free). *Ṭayyib* (good, wholesome): nature-pure (clean, unpolluted), body-beneficial (nourishing, non-harmful — *"no harm,"* Ibn Mājah 2340), spirit-suitable (God-consciousness aiding). *The duality*: ḥalāl-without-ṭayyib (lawful-but-harmful: lawful food excessively eaten — gluttony; lawful earnings anxiously hoarded), ṭayyib-without-ḥalāl (wholesome-but-forbidden: pork-protein, wine-medicine — barred), ḥalāl-ṭayyib-together (the ideal: lawful *and* wholesome — v. 88's commanded). *Beyond diet*: ḥalāl-ṭayyib wealth (lawfully-earned, purely-spent), ḥalāl-ṭayyib speech (truthful, gentle), ḥalāl-ṭayyib company (believing, elevating). Eat doubly-qualified; live doubly-qualified. Lawful guards conscience; wholesome guards body. Both commanded; both blessed.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:89

> **God will not take you to task for what is unintentional in your oaths, but He will take you to task for what you have contracted by oaths. The expiation thereof is feeding ten indigent people with the average of what you feed your families, or clothing them, or freeing a slave. But whosoever finds not [the means], then a fast of three days. That is the expiation of your oaths when you swear. And keep your oaths. Thus does God make clear His signs unto you, that haply you may be thankful.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Laghw in Oaths: The Unintentional Pardoned**

*God takes you not to task for laghw* (*laghw* — idle, unintentional: habitual "by God"-utterances, slips, oaths on mistaken belief (Bḍ, Ṭ, Z; cf. 2:225)) *in oaths* — Divine indulgence toward human speech-habits: tongues outrunning intentions pardoned (contrast deliberate *jahd*-oaths — v. 53's strenuous swearing). *But He takes to task for what you contracted* (*ʿaqqadtum* — tied, bound, deliberate) *by oaths* — deliberate pledges binding (intention tying tongue). The distinction founds oath-jurisprudence: unintentional void (no expiation), intentional binding (fulfill-or-expiate). *Keep your oaths* (*iḥfaẓū aymānakum* — guard: fulfill-or-expiate, swear-sparingly) — oath-economy: swear rarely (2:224: God-not-obstacle), fulfill faithfully (16:91: covenant-completion), expiate promptly (this verse). The verse disciplines tongues: idle pardoned, deliberate binding, all guarded. Swear little; mean much; keep all.

**Kaffārat al-Yamīn: Oath-Expiation's Ladder**

*Fa-kaffāratuhu iṭʿāmu ʿasharati masākīna... aw kiswatuhum... aw taḥrīru raqabah... fa-man lam yajid fa-ṣiyāmu thalāthati ayyām* — expiation's *ladder*: feed-ten (average family-food — *awsaṭ*, middle-standard), clothe-ten, free-a-slave — *or* (means-lacking) fast-three-days. *The ladder's wisdom*: wealth-expiations first (social benefit — poor fed, clothed, freed — sin's price *feeding* society), bodily-expiation last (fasting — private discipline). *The standards*: *awsaṭ* (average — neither miserly-minimum nor boastful-maximum — moderation legislated), ten-persons (community-scale — expiation *social*), three-days (consecutive per some — discipline sustained). *The parallels*: ẓihār-expiation (58:3–4: free-fast-feed — graver sin, steeper ladder), hunting-expiation (v. 95: equivalent-or-food-or-fast), ḥajj-expiation (2:196). *The theology*: oaths *breakable-lawfully* (expiation-gated — better-course permitted: *"whoever swears then sees better, let him expiate and do better,"* Muslim 1650) — rigidity's veto, mercy’s procedure. Break rightly (expiate), swear rightly (guard), fulfill rightly (complete). Ladder climbed; oaths kept; gratitude due.

**"That You May Be Thankful": Legislation's Gratitude-Aim**

*Ka-dhālika yubayyinu'Llāhu lakum āyātihi laʿallakum tashkurūn* — "thus God clarifies signs, that you may thank": legislation's *gratitude-aim* — laws *clarified* (oaths ruled, expiations laddered) *for* thankfulness. *The logic*: clarity enabling gratitude (rules known → obedience possible → blessings recognized → thanks offered). *Shukr* (thankfulness) as legislation's *telos*: oaths-gifted (commitment-tools), expiations-provided (failure-remedies), pardons-granted (*laghw* excused) — each bounty thanking. *The ungrateful* (*kaffār*-oaths): swearing-falsely (oaths weaponized), breaking-carelessly (expiations ignored), swearing-excessively (2:224's obstacle-oaths). *Laʿallakum* (that-you-may): legislation *inviting* (not compelling) gratitude — clarity offered, thanks hoped. Know rules; keep oaths; expiate breaches; thank the Clarifier. Legislation's aim: thankful obedience. Signs clarified; gratitude due; thanks offered.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:90

> **O you who believe! Wine, gambling, idols, and divining arrows are but a defilement of Satan's doing. So avoid them, that haply you may prosper.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Rijs of Satan's Doing: Four Defilements Banned**

*Wine* (*khamr*), *gambling* (*maysir*), *idols* (*anṣāb* — sacrificial stones), *divining arrows* (*azlām* — lot-arrows) — *rijs* (*rijs* — defilement, abomination) *of Satan's doing* (*min ʿamali'sh-shayṭān*) — four defilements, one author (Satan), one verdict (avoid). *Khamr* (wine — covering-intellect, from *khamara*: to cover): all intoxicants (*"every intoxicant is khamr, every khamr forbidden,"* Muslim 2003; *"whatever intoxicates largely, its little is forbidden,"* Abū Dāwūd 3681). *Maysir* (gambling — from *yusr*, ease: wealth-without-toil): all zero-sum wagering (bets, lotteries, speculative *gharar*-excesses). *Anṣāb* (idol-stones): pagan altars (v. 3's dedicated-slaughter parallel). *Azlām* (divining arrows): fortune-lots (seeking decisions from randomness — *istikhārah*'s pagan counterfeit). *Avoid them* (*ijtanibūh* — shun entirely: production, sale, transport, profit — *"cursed ten for wine,"* al-Tirmidhī 1295) — *that you may prosper* (*tufliḥūn* — *falāḥ*'s harvest, cf. v. 35). Four banned; Satan authored; prosperity conditioned.

**Khamr: Wine's Gradual Prohibition**

*Innama'l-khamru... rijsun* — wine *abominated* — the *culmination* of gradual prohibition (*tadrīj*), and the gradation's wisdom needs tracing. *Stage one* (Makkan): wine *mentioned* neutrally-descriptively (16:67: *"from fruits, intoxicant and good provision"* — juxtaposition hinting). *Stage two* (early Madinan): wine *questioned* (2:219: *"in both is great sin and some benefit; sin greater"* — cost-benefit revealed). *Stage three* (mid-Madinan): wine *restricted* (4:43: *"approach not prayer intoxicated"* — sobriety-zones expanding). *Stage four* (late Madinan — this verse): wine *prohibited* absolutely (*ijtanibūh* — avoid; *rijs* — defilement; Satanic). *The Ummah's response*: streets flowing (wine-skins emptied at announcement — al-Bukhārī 2464; Muslim 1980 — Madinah's gutters wine-filled), obedience instant (*"we desisted, Lord!"* — sahābah's cry). *The wisdom*: addiction's graduality (habits unbuilt stepwise), conviction's building (evidences accumulating), obedience's training (restrictions accepted progressively). Legislate wisely (gradual where addictive), obey instantly (at prohibition's arrival). Gradual revealed; instant obeyed; streets flowed.

**Maysir, Anṣāb, Azlām: The Other Three**

*Maysir* (gambling), *anṣāb* (idol-stones), *azlām* (divining arrows) — the *other three* defilements — each needs its contemporary mapping. *Maysir* (ease-wealth — gain-without-toil): classical (bets, wagers, *qimār*), modern (casinos, lotteries, speculative day-trading-as-gambling, loot-boxes, prediction-markets-as-wagers) — zero-sum wealth-transfers (winners' gains = losers' losses, society impoverished net). *Anṣāb* (sacrificial stones — idol-altars): classical (pagan slaughter-sites), modern (ideology-altars — wealth worshipped, nation deified, celebrities adored — slaughter-sites of time-and-soul). *Azlām* (divining arrows — decision-lots): classical (arrow-shuffled verdicts — *"do/not-do"* lots), modern (horoscopes, fortune-apps, decision-outsourced-to-randomness — *istikhārah*-and-*shūrā*'s counterfeits). *Min ʿamali'sh-shayṭān* (Satan's handiwork): all four Satan-authored (enmity-tools — v. 91; remembrance-barriers — v. 91). *Ijtanibūh* (avoid-all): total shunning (production-through-profit chains — the ten-cursed ḥadīth). Map modernly; avoid totally; prosper consequently.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:91

> **Satan desires only to cast enmity and hatred among you through wine and gambling, and to bar you from the remembrance of God and from prayer. Will you not, then, desist?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Satan's Two Aims: Enmity and Remembrance-Barred**

*Satan desires only* (*innamā yurīdu*) *to cast enmity* (*ʿadāwah*) *and hatred* (*baghḍā'*) *among you through wine-and-gambling, and bar you from God's remembrance* (*dhikr*) *and prayer* (*ṣalāh*) — Satan's *dual* strategy: horizontal (enmity-between-brothers) and vertical (barrier-before-God). Wine's enmity: tongues loosened (secrets spilled, insults hurled), fists emboldened (brawls ignited), wealth squandered (families ruined) — Ḥamzah's drunken camel-maiming (the *sīrah*'s prohibition-occasion — al-Bukhārī 2089) epitomizing. Gambling's hatred: winners gloated-over, losers ruined (debts, thefts, suicides — hatred's harvests). Remembrance barred: intoxicants *covering* (*khamara*) intellect (God unremembered), gambling *consuming* (prayer-times wagered-away). *Will you not desist* (*a-fa-antum muntahūn*) — the wooing-question (cf. v. 74's *a-fa-lā yatūbūn*): desistance *demanded*, response *awaited*. The Companions answered: *"We desist, Lord!"* (*intahaynā Rabbanā* — Abū Dāwūd 3670; al-Tirmidhī 3049). Enmity sown; remembrance barred; desistance demanded.

**ʿAdāwah and Baghḍā': Enmity Versus Hatred**

*An yūqiʿa baynakumu'l-ʿadāwata wa'l-baghḍā'a* — "casting among you enmity and hatred": the *paired* afflictions — *ʿadāwah* (enmity, active hostility) and *baghḍā'* (hatred, inward loathing) — and the pairing's psychology needs parsing. *ʿAdāwah* (from *ʿ-d-w*: crossing-over): *active* aggression (assaults, lawsuits, feuds — hostility *acted*). *Baghḍā'* (from *b-gh-ḍ*: loathing): *affective* aversion (resentments, grudges, contempt — hostility *felt*). *The sequence*: wine-and-gambling → *baghḍā'* (losers hating winners, families hating squanderers, victims hating brawlers) → *ʿadāwah* (hatred *acting*: violence, litigation, rupture). *The Qur'anic pairing* (5:14, 64; 60:4 — enmity-and-hatred *between* communities): social-fabric shredded (brotherhood — 49:10 — dissolved, affection — 30:21; 8:63 — reversed). *Satan's authorship* (*innamā yurīdu'sh-shayṭān* — *only* Satan desires): enmity-hatred *exclusively* Satanic (God desires brotherhood — 3:103; 49:10; Satan desires rupture — 17:53: *"Satan sows discord"*). Desire brotherhood (Divine), suspect rupture (Satanic), desist from sowers (wine-gambling). Pair parsed; author exposed; desistance urged.

**Ṣadd ʿan adh-Dhikr: Barred from Remembrance**

*Wa yaṣuddakum ʿan dhikri'Llāhi wa ʿani'ṣ-ṣalāh* — "barring you from God's remembrance and prayer": *ṣadd* (barring, obstructing) — Satan's *vertical* strategy — and remembrance-barriers need their inventory. *Wine's barring*: intellect *covered* (remembrance unremembered — drunkards God-forgetting), prayer *approached-intoxicated* (4:43's pre-prohibition restriction — comprehension voided), worship *abandoned* (alcoholics prayer-negligent). *Gambling's barring*: time *consumed* (sessions swallowing prayer-times), wealth *dissipated* (zakāh-charity unfunded), hearts *agitated* (stakes-anxiety crowding tranquility — *sakīnah*'s opposite). *The remembrance-prayer pair* (*dhikr-ṣalāh*): remembrance *general* (all God-consciousness — Qur'an, duʿā', reflection), prayer *specific* (remembrance's ritual-peak — 20:14: *"establish prayer for My remembrance"*). *Ṣadd*'s agents-generalized: all intoxicants (drugs, alcohol), all addictions (gambling, gaming, scrolling — prayer-times consumed), all distractions (worldliness crowding worship). Unbar remembrance (sober, attentive, present); unbar prayer (timely, focused, complete). *A-fa-antum muntahūn* — desisting? Answer: *intahaynā* — we desist.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:92

> **And obey God and obey the Messenger, and beware! But if you turn away, then know that upon Our Messenger is only the clear conveyance.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Obey and Beware: The Prohibition's Enforcement Clause**

*Obey God and obey the Messenger* (*aṭīʿū'Llāha wa aṭīʿū'r-Rasūl*) *and beware* (*iḥdharū* — guard against violation) — the prohibition's (vv. 90–91) *enforcement clause*: obedience commanded doubly (God-and-Messenger — cf. 3:32; 4:59; 24:54), vigilance added (*beware* — neither wine-nor-gambling approached). *If you turn away* (*tawallaytum*) — violation threatened — *then know: upon Our Messenger is only clear conveyance* (*balāgh mubīn* — cf. v. 67's *balligh*; 16:35, 82; 24:54; 29:18; 64:12). The clause's logic: enforcement *tiered* — Divine command (obey), prophetic conveyance (warned), violator liability (turning-away self-sentenced). The Messenger *conveys* (responsibility discharged); violators *bear* (consequences owned). *Balāgh mubīn* (clear conveyance): clarity *achieved* (prohibition unmistakable — *rijs*, *ijtanibūh*, Satanic), excuses *voided* (turning-away inexcusable). Obey doubly; beware singly; turn never. Conveyed clearly; liability personally.

**Ṭāʿah: The Double Obedience**

*Aṭīʿū'Llāha wa aṭīʿū'r-Rasūla* — "obey God and obey the Messenger": the *double* obedience — and its structure governs all authority-relations. *The repetitions*: commanded multiply (3:32; 4:59; 5:92; 8:1, 20, 46; 24:54; 47:33; 58:13; 64:12) — obedience's liturgy. *The structure*: God obeyed (Qur'an followed), Messenger obeyed (Sunnah followed — 4:80: *"obeying Messenger is obeying God"*; 59:7: *"take what Messenger gives"*) — two channels, one stream (revelation). *The extension*: authorities obeyed (4:59's *ulū'l-amr* — rulers, scholars — *within* God-Messenger obedience; *"no obedience in disobedience to God,"* al-Bukhārī 7257; Muslim 1840). *The beware* (*iḥdharū*): vigilance *added* (obedience *guarded* — neither laxity nor excess: wine avoided-totally, oaths kept-carefully, bounds honored-precisely). *The turning* (*tawallaytum*): disobedience *threatened* (liability personal — Messenger's conveyance discharging *him*, condemning *turners*). Obey doubly (God-Messenger), extend conditionally (authorities-within), beware perpetually (vigilance), turn never (liability). Double commanded; vigilance added; clarity achieved.

**Balāgh Mubīn: Conveyance Discharging**

*Fa'ʿlamū annamā ʿalā Rasūlinā'l-balāghu'l-mubīn* — "know: upon Our Messenger is only clear conveyance": *balāgh* (conveyance) *discharging* — responsibility's *limit* — and the limit liberates warners while binding hearers. *The limit*: messengers *convey* (proclaim clearly — v. 67's *balligh*), never *compel* (guidance unforced — 2:256; 10:99; 88:21–22: *"remind — thou art only a reminder, no compeller"*), never *convert* (hearts God's — 28:56). *The clarity* (*mubīn*): conveyance *manifest* (prohibitions unmistakable — *rijs*-declared, *ijtanibūh*-commanded, Satanic-exposed), excuses *demolished* (turning-away deliberate — knowledge established). *The discharge*: Messenger *acquitted* (duty done — *"have I conveyed?"* — Farewell Sermon; *"O God, witness"*), hearers *liable* (*fa'ʿlamū* — *know*: consequences yours). *The inheritance*: warners conveying (scholars, preachers, parents — clarity-owed), never compelling (coercion-barred), discharged-upon-delivery (results God's — vv. 41, 64, 68's consolations). Convey clearly; compel never; discharged thereby. *Balāgh mubīn* — clarity liberating warners, binding hearers.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:93

> **There is no blame upon those who believe and work righteousness for what they have tasted, when they are reverent and believe and work righteousness, then are reverent and believe, then are reverent and do good. And God loves the virtuous.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**No Blame for Past Tasting: The Retrospective Pardon**

*No blame* (*junāḥ* — sin, liability) *on believers-working-righteousness for what they tasted* (*ṭaʿimū* — consumed pre-prohibition: wine drunk, gambling played before vv. 90–91) — the retrospective pardon: prohibitions *prospective* (binding-forward), never *retroactive* (condemning-backward). The occasion: Companions worrying over deceased wine-drinkers (Uḥud-martyrs dying pre-prohibition with wine in veins — Ṭ, W) and over own past consumption — revelation absolving: pre-prohibition tasting blameless (prohibition un-arrived, liability un-attached). *When they are reverent-and-believe-and-work-righteous, then reverent-and-believe, then reverent-and-do-good* — the *triple* progression (taqwā-īmān-ʿamal → taqwā-īmān → taqwā-iḥsān): ascending stations (belief practicing, practicing believing, believing excelling). *God loves muḥsinīn* (virtuous — cf. v. 85's *jazā'*, 55:60) — love crowning excellence. Past pardoned; progression commanded; excellence loved.

**Lā Junāḥa: Non-Retroactivity of Prohibition**

*Laysa ʿala'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿamilu'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti junāḥun fī-mā ṭaʿimū* — "no blame on believers-righteous for what they tasted": prohibition's *non-retroactivity* — and the principle's justice needs stating. *The principle*: laws bind *forward* (post-promulgation conduct liable), never *backward* (pre-promulgation conduct excused — 17:15's messenger-before-punishment; 4:22–23's past-marriages excepted — *"save what passed"*). *The applications*: wine-drunk-pre-90 (blameless — Uḥud-martyrs secure), interest-taken-pre-prohibition (2:275's *"past remitted"*), pagan-acts-pre-Islam (converts' pages whitened — 8:38: *"past forgiven"*; 25:70's sins-converted). *The conditions* (this verse): belief-and-righteousness *maintained* (pardon for *believers-practicing*, not rebels-persisting), reverence *progressing* (triple ascent — see below), excellence *reached* (love-earning). *The pastoral*: converts agonizing over pagan-pasts (absolved — 8:38), repentants over pre-repentance sins (covered — 39:53), worriers over deceased-pre-prohibition (secure — this verse's martyrs). Blame never retroactive; pardon always prospective-conditional. Tasted-then blameless; tasting-now liable. Progress upward; loved thereby.

**Triple Taqwā: The Ascending Stations**

*Idha'ma'ttaqaw wa āmanū wa ʿamilu'ṣ-ṣāliḥāti thumma'ttaqaw wa āmanū thumma'ttaqaw wa aḥsanū* — "when reverent-believing-working, then reverent-believing, then reverent-excelling": the *triple* taqwā — ascending stations — and the ascent needs mapping. *Station one* (*ittaqaw-āmanū-ʿamilū*): reverence *founding* (fear restraining), belief *informing* (conviction guiding), righteousness *performing* (deeds doing) — Islām practiced. *Station two* (*thumma'ttaqaw-āmanū*): reverence *deepening* (*thumma* — then, progression), belief *strengthening* (certainty growing — *yaqīn*'s ascent) — īmān matured. *Station three* (*thumma'ttaqaw-aḥsanū*): reverence *perfecting*, excellence *achieving* (*iḥsān* — worship-as-seeing — Muslim 8) — iḥsān attained. *The trajectory*: practice → conviction → excellence (doing → believing-deeply → excelling) — taqwā *constant* (all three stations), companions *ascending* (deeds → faith → excellence). *Wa'Llāhu yuḥibbu'l-muḥsinīn* (God loves excelling): love *terminating* ascent (excellence beloved). Ascend station-wise: practice reverently, believe deeply, excel lovingly. Triple commanded; love promised; ascent endless.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:94

> **O you who believe! God shall surely try you with something of the game that your hands and spears can reach, that God may know who fears Him in the unseen. So whosoever transgresses after that, his shall be a painful punishment.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Game Within Reach: The Ḥudaybiyah Trial**

*God shall surely try you* (*la-yabluwannakum*) *with game* (*ṣayd*) *hands-and-spears reaching* — the Ḥudaybiyah trial (6/628): pilgrims in *iḥrām* (hunting-forbidden — v. 1's *lā taḥillū'ṣ-ṣayda*; v. 95) surrounded by tame game (animals approaching fearlessly — Ṭ, W), temptation *maximal* (hunger, ease, privacy), prohibition *binding* (iḥrām-restriction). *That God may know* (*li-yaʿlama* — manifest, demonstrate) *who fears Him unseen* (*bi'l-ghayb* — fearing-invisible-God, resisting-invisible-to-men — no ranger watching, only Heaven). *Transgressing after that* — hunting-despite (warning received, trial clarified) — *painful punishment*. The trial's genius: prohibition *easiest-to-violate* (game abundant, detection impossible) testing *unseen-fear* purely (restraint Godward-only). Iḥrām's hunting-ban (vv. 1, 94–96) trains creation-reverence: pilgrims-consumers becoming pilgrims-protectors (life spared at hunger's peak). Tried by ease; proven by restraint; punished for transgression.

**Li-Yaʿlama: Testing That Manifests**

*Li-yaʿlama'Llāhu man yakhāfuhū bi'l-ghayb* — "that God may know who fears Him unseen": Divine *testing-for-manifestation* — and the *li-yaʿlama* (that-He-may-know) needs theology's care (God omniscient — testing manifesting, not informing). *The function*: trials *demonstrating* (hidden-fear evidenced — restraint proving reverence), *distinguishing* (fearers separated from violators — 3:179's bad-good sifted), *recording* (deeds witnessed — angels scribing, limbs testifying). *The unseen-fear* (*khashyah bi'l-ghayb*): fearing-invisible-God (35:18; 67:12: *"fearing Lord unseen — forgiveness and great reward"*), resisting-invisible-to-men (privacy's test — no watcher but God), sincerity's proof (restraint unobserved-unrewarded-earthly). *The trial's design*: temptation-maximized (game tame, hunger real, detection nil), prohibition-clarified (iḥrām-law known), restraint-measured (hands withheld, spears lowered). *Qur'anic parallels*: Ṭālūt's river (2:249 — drinking-forbidden, discipline-tested), Sabbath-fish (7:163 — Saturday-abundant, restraint-tested), Ḥudaybiyah-game (this verse). Test manifesting; fear evidencing; restraint rewarding. *Li-yaʿlama* — known-manifested, fearers-honored.

**Iḥrām's Hunting Ban: Creation-Reverence Trained**

*Ṣayd* (game, hunting) hands-reaching yet forbidden — iḥrām's hunting-ban (vv. 1, 94–96) — and the ban's *creation-theology* needs drawing. *The ban*: land-game forbidden-in-iḥrām (v. 95's penalties; v. 96's sea-game permitted), Ḥaram-game forbidden-perpetually (sanctuary's inviolability —27:91; 29:67), trees-and-plants protected (Ḥaram-vegetation uncut — al-Bukhārī 112; Muslim 1353). *The training*: pilgrims *consumers-becoming-protectors* (hunger-endured, life-spared — appetite subordinated), violence *renounced-comprehensively* (no hunting, no harming, no uprooting — peace *worn* with iḥrām-garments), creation *reverenced-sacramentally* (animals-as-signs — 6:38's communities; 24:41's worshippers — spared as worshippers). *The theology*: creation's *sanctity* (life God-given — v. 32's soul-sanctity extended), humanity's *trusteeship* (khalīfah-tending — 2:30; 7:56's corruption-forbidden), worship's *cosmicity* (all creation worshipping — 17:44; 22:18 — pilgrims joining, not interrupting). *Man iʿtadā baʿda dhālik* (transgressing-after): violation *knowing* (trial-clarified, ban-known) — painful-punishment earned. Spare game; revere creation; fear unseen. Iḥrām training; Ḥaram modeling; earth deserving.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:95

> **O you who believe! Slay not the game while you are in pilgrim sanctity. And whosoever among you slays it deliberately, then the recompense is the like thereof among the flocks, to be judged by two just men among you, as an offering delivered to the Kaʿbah, or an expiation of feeding the indigent, or the equivalent thereof in fasting, that he may taste the consequences of his deed. God has pardoned what is past, but whosoever reverts, God will take vengeance upon him. And God is Mighty, Lord of Vengeance.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Slaying Game in Iḥrām: The Violation Priced**

*Slay not game while in pilgrim sanctity* (*ḥurum* — iḥrām-state) — the prohibition restated absolutely (cf. vv. 1, 94). *Deliberate slaying* (*mutaʿammidan* — intentional; mistakes per some exempted (Ṭ) or equally liable (Q)) — *recompense: the like* (*mithl*) *among flocks* (*naʿam* — camels, cattle, sheep) — equivalence-judged (ostrich→camel, gazelle→sheep, per Companion-precedents). *Judged by two just men* (*dhawā ʿadlin* — justice-possessors) — peer-adjudication (community-justice, expertise-consulted). *Offering delivered to Kaʿbah* (*hadyan bāligha'l-kaʿbah* — slaughtered at Ḥaram, meat-distributed to poor) — violation-funding-charity. *Or feeding indigent* (*kaffārah ṭaʿām masākīn* — equivalent-value meals) *or equivalent fasting* (*ʿadl dhālika ṣiyāman* — day-per-meal) — ladder-options (wealth-or-body). *That he may taste consequences* (*wabāl* — evil outcome, burden) — punishment *pedagogical* (tasting-teaching). Violation priced; justice peered; consequences tasted.

**Ḥakamayn ʿAdlayn: Two Just Arbiters**

*Yaḥkumu bihī dhawā ʿadlin minkum* — "two just men among you judging it": peer-*tribunal* for game-valuation — and the tribunal's jurisprudence seeds Islamic *arbitration*-law. *The judges*: *dhawā ʿadl* (justice-possessors — upright, knowledgeable — game-experts consulted, values assessed), *minkum* (among-you — community-internal, peer-legitimated). *The judgment*: *mithl*-determination (like-for-like — species-matched, value-assessed — Companion-precedents: ʿUmar-ʿUthmān-ʿAlī game-valuations transmitted), offering-or-feeding-or-fasting (options-priced). *The parallels*: marital-arbitration (4:35's *ḥakaman* — family-judges), commercial-arbitration (2:282's witnesses; dispute-resolution Sunnah), *taḥkīm*-institution (arbitration Sunnah-rooted — *"arbitration is..."* traditions). *The wisdom*: community-justice (peers judging — legitimacy-local), expertise-valued (hunters-and-scholars consulting), restoration-prioritized (offering-feeding — violation funding charity). Judge peer-wise; value expertly; restore charitably. *Ḥakamayn* judging; *ʿadl* possessing; *mithl* delivering.

**ʿAfā/Naqam: Pardon Past, Vengeance for Reversion**

*ʿAfā'Llāhu ʿammā salaf, wa man ʿāda fa-yantaqimu'Llāhu minh* — "God pardoned the past; reverting, God avenges": pardon-and-vengeance *paired* — mercy-for-past, severity-for-relapse. *Pardon* (*ʿafw* — effacing: pre-clarification violations wiped — Ḥudaybiyah-era mistakes, ignorance-mitigated). *Reversion* (*ʿawd* — returning-to-violation *after* clarification): knowledge-established (ban known, trial clarified — v. 94), pardon-received (past wiped), violation-repeated (ingratitude-compounding) — vengeance-earned. *Vengeance* (*intiqām* — retributive justice; *Dhū'ntiqām*, Lord-of-Vengeance — 3:4; 5:95; 14:47; 39:37; 43:41): Divine *severity* (not temper — judicial requital), *Mighty*-paired (*ʿAzīz* — irresistible execution). *The pattern*: pardon-then-warning (past wiped, future threatened — 2:275's interest; 8:38's converts; this verse's hunters), relapse-then-vengeance (clarity spurned, pardon abused). *Wa'Llāhu ʿAzīzun Dhūntiqām* (Mighty, Vengeance-Lord): the Names *sealing* (severity's ground — Might executing, Vengeance requiting). Pardoned once; warned clearly; reverting-avenged. Past effaced; future guarded; vengeance certain-for-persisters.

---

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:96

> **Permitted unto you is the game of the sea and the food thereof, as a provision for you and for travelers. But forbidden unto you is the game of the land, so long as you are in pilgrim sanctity. And reverence God, unto Whom you shall be gathered.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Sea Game Permitted: The Concession's Latitude**

*Permitted is sea-game* (*ṣayd al-baḥr* — fishing, sea-hunting) *and its food* (*ṭaʿāmuh* — sea-food, whether caught-by-pilgrims or found/presented) — *provision for residents-and-travelers* (*matāʿan lakum wa li's-sayyārah* — sustenance universal). The concession's latitude: iḥrām restricting land (vv. 94–95), liberating sea — pilgrims' protein secured (Red-Sea coasts, Jeddah-fisheries feeding Ḥajj-caravans), travelers provisioned (*sayyārah* — caravans, voyagers). *Land-game forbidden in iḥrām* (*ḥurum*) — restated (third time — vv. 1, 95, 96: emphasis's insistence). *Reverence God, unto Whom gathered* (*tuḥsharūn* — resurrection-assembly) — taqwā *eschatologically* grounded (gathering-certain, hunting-judged). Sea liberated; land restricted; gathering certain. Provision's geography; reverence's horizon.

**Baḥr Versus Barr: Sea Liberated, Land Restricted**
